From the trials, the LSTM model showed the lowest R oot 
Mean Square Error (RMSE) value, thus it was selecte d as the 
best representation of the deep learning-based appr oach’s 
performance. This model was then compared with the baseline 
7-day Moving Average (MA(7)) method, which reflects  the 
weekly purchasing practices of small businesses. Th e 
comparison results showed that the optimized LSTM 
produced an RMSE of 1.6500, lower than the MA(7) of  
1.8390, with a slightly smaller MAPE. The relativel y high 
MAPE values are partly influenced by the presence o f 
multiple zero-transaction days in the SME dataset. When 
actual demand values are very small or zero, percen tage-based 
error metrics tend to become inflated. 
From the experimental tests, the best LSTM model wa s 
achieved using a 30-day time window, a single LSTM layer 
with 16 neuron units, and a dropout rate of 0.2. Al though this 
architecture is relatively simple and does not use a deep 
network structure, the test results show an increas e in accuracy 
compared to the application of a moving average. 
Statistically, the optimized LSTM model demonstrate d a 
better ability to capture daily demand patterns tha n MA(7), as 
reflected in its lower error value. From an operati onal 
perspective, MA(7) resulted in a shortage of 2,286 units 
during the test period, potentially leading to lost  sales. In 
contrast, LSTM was able to eliminate the shortage, albeit at 
the cost of 1,573 units of excess stock. This diffe rence 
suggests that improving forecast accuracy can have a direct 
impact on the quality of inventory decisions, parti cularly in 
balancing the risks of overstocking and understocki ng in small 
businesses. Future work may incorporate such heuris tics, such 
as Naive persistence models, to further evaluate th e relative 
performance of deep learning approaches in SME dema nd 
forecasting contexts. 
V.  CONCLUSION  
This study aims to confirm whether an LSTM-based sa les 
forecasting method exhibits better performance than  the 7-day 
Moving Average method in the context of inventory d ecision-
making for small businesses that rely on daily sale s data. The 
findings indicate that the LSTM model demonstrates 
statistically superior performance, although this i mprovement 
does not directly lead to more efficient inventory decisions. 
The LSTM improvements only truly emerge with consis tency 
Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 06,2026 at 19:29:43 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
in both statistical and operational terms after par ameter 
optimization using a grid search approach. The weak nesses of 
this study are evident in its focus on a single pro duct type and 
the limited variety of forecasting models tested. A nother 
limitation of this study lies in the short evaluati on window and 
the focus on a single product. Furthermore, invento ry 
evaluation is still based on a simple unit-based ap proach 
without explicitly considering cost dynamics. There fore, 
future research should include more items with diff erent 
demand characteristics, test other relevant forecas ting models, 
and apply alternative optimization methods to gain a more 
comprehensive understanding of the relationship bet ween 
forecast accuracy and operational efficiency.

 Forecasting Daily Sales of Coffee Shop Products: A 
Comparative Study of Moving Average, ARIMA, 
and SARIMA   
1stAulia Azzahra   
Entrepreneurship Department 
BINUS Business School  
Undergraduate Program  
Bina Nusantara University 
Bandung, Indonesia, 40181  
aulia.azzahra @binus.ac.id  
  
 
 2ndIvan Diryana Sudirman  
Entrepreneurship Department 
BINUS Business School 
Undergraduate Program  
Bina Nusantara University 
Bandung, Indonesia, 40181  
ivan.diryana @binus.ac.id 
 
Abstract —The coffee shop industry in Indonesia has 
experienced rapid growth, with local brands like Kopi Janji 
Jiwa and Kopi Kenangan expanding aggressively. However, 
coffee shops often face challenges in demand forecasting, leading 
to inventory mismanagement and operational inefficiencies. 
This study focuses on forecasting daily sales of Ice Afecto, a 
popular coffee shop product, using time series models to 
enhance business decision-making. This study compare the 
performance of three forecasting methods Moving Average 
(MA), Autoregressive Integrated Moving Average (ARIMA), 
and Seasonal ARIMA (SARIMA) to identify the most accurate 
model for short-term sales prediction. Utilizing transaction data 
from a coffee shop, processed and analyzed to capture daily sales 
trends. Results indicate that ARIMA (0,0,1) (0,0,1) outperforms 
both MA and SARIMA, achieving the lowest Mean Absolute 
Error and Root Mean Squared Error. While MA provides 
simplicity, it fails to capture rapid fluctuations, whereas 
SARIMA underperforms due to weak seasonal patterns in the 
data. The study highlights ARIMA’s effectiveness in optimizing 
inventory management, workforce scheduling, and promotional 
strategies, offering practical benefits for small-to-medium coffee 
shops. This research contributes to the industry by 
demonstrating how data-driven forecasting can minimize waste, 
improve resource allocation, and enhance profitability. Future 
work should incorporate external factors such as weather and 
promotions to further refine predictions. The findings provide 
valuable insights for coffee shop operators seeking to implement 
predictive analytics in their daily operations.  
Keywords—coffee shop industry, time series analysis, ARIMA, 
inventory optimization, business intelligence 
I. INTRODUCTION  
Coffee is a vital plantation commodity for Indonesia, as 
this commodity serves as a small-scale farmers’ income 
source, offers industrial raw materials, plays a role in rural 
development for sustainable agriculture, and contributes 
considerably to export earnings [1]. Right now there are now 
plenty of prospects for business owners to open coffee shops 
thanks to the expansion of the tourism industry and the rising 
trend of coffee consumption [2]. The coffee shop industry has 
become an integral part of modern urban life. Today, coffee 
shops serve as hubs for social interaction, entrepreneurship, 
and community development. With the rise of coffee 
enthusiasts ranging from teenagers to the elderly, along with 
the growing demand for specialty coffee and the desire for 
unique and personalized experiences, the coffee shop industry 
has evolved to meet consumers' ever-changing preferences[3]. 
Currently, data-driven business decision-making in modern 
operations has propelled data analytics and business 
intelligence, along with performance management solutions, to become fundamental organizational tools for strategic 
marketing success [4].  
The figure demonstrates the rapid growth of Indonesia's 
coffee shop industry, with Kopi Janji Jiwa and Kopi  Kenangan 
having the highest number of outlets in 2022 - even surpassing 
global chains like KFC and Pizza Hut. This reflects both the 
rising trend of coffee consumption and the aggressive 
expansion of local brands. 
Coffee shops frequently face challenges in predicting 
product demand, which can lead to inventory management 
issues such as overstocking or stockouts. This problem 
becomes increasingly complex due to daily sales fluctuations 
influenced by various variables, including weekday/weekend 
patterns, seasonal factors, and the impact of promotions or 
discounts. These conditions create a need to identify more 
sophisticated and accurate forecasting models that can assist 
coffee shop operators in both day-to-day operational decision-
making and developing more effective long-term business 
strategies. Proper implementation of forecasting systems will 
enable management to optimize raw material inventory, 
schedule staff shifts more efficiently, and plan promotions 
more precisely based on actual sales data. 
This study focuses on forecasting daily sales of Ice Afecto, 
a popular product, using time series models. The objectives 
are: 
• To analyze daily sales patterns of Ice Afecto. 
• To compare the performance of Moving Average, 
ARIMA, and SARIMA models. 
• To determine the best forecasting model for supporting 
business decisions. 
The choice between Moving Average (MA), ARIMA, and 
SARIMA depends on data characteristics. MA works best for 
simple trend smoothing, ARIMA handles non-seasonal 
trends, while SARIMA captures seasonal patterns. Comparing 
these models is crucial because businesses need to balance 
accuracy with practicality - complex models may overfit while 
simple ones may underfit. For inventory management and 
staffing decisions, selecting the right model ensures cost-
effective and reliable forecasts. This study helps small 
businesses choose the most suitable forecasting method for 
their specific operational needs. The research contributes to 
both academia and industry by providing a detailed analysis 
of sales patterns and evaluating forecasting methods tailored 
for small to medium-sized coffee shops. 2025 International Conference on Data Science and Its Applications (ICoDSA)

II. LITERATURE REVIEW  
A. Time Series Forecasting in Business 
Sales forecasting is crucial in the food and beverage 
industry, enabling businesses to optimize inventory and refine 
marketing strategies. Time series forecasting, a key research 
area in data science, involves preprocessing, sequence 
modeling, prediction, and aggregation [5]. Large 
observational time-series data sets are usually accessible in 
these domains, but large-scale studies to ascertain causal 
effects are generally difficult or unattainable. Therefore, 
creating statistical techniques that use large datasets to 
determine causal links is a crucial issue [6]. This method 
analyzes historical patterns to predict future trends while 
accounting for temporal factors. Statistical approaches (e.g., 
ARIMA) perform well with limited data, whereas machine 
learning (e.g., LSTM) excels with complex patterns in large 
datasets. 
B. Arima Model in Forecasting 
Recent studies highlight the effectiveness of ARIMA 
models in forecasting perishable goods sales, particularly in 
the agricultural and retail sectors. For instance, research on 
vegetable sales demonstrates that seasonal ARIMA, combined 
with first-order differencing, can accurately predict weekly 
replenishment needs by accounting for trends and seasonal 
fluctuations [7]. This approach is especially valuable for 
perishable items like vegetables, where demand variability 
and loss rates significantly impact pricing and inventory 
decisions. 
C. Optimizing Inventory Management with ARIMA 
Forecasting 
Traditional time series models, such as ARIMA, have 
been widely employed for midterm daily load forecasting due 
to their ability to capture temporal dependencies and trends in 
historical data. Recent advancements include hybrid 
approaches like Wavelet-ARIMA, which decomposes load 
data into frequency components to enhance forecasting 
accuracy. Studies demonstrate that wavelet decomposition 
improves model performance by isolating underlying patterns 
and reducing noise [8]. However, these models often overlook 
external factors, such as climatic conditions, which can 
significantly influence load demand. 
This methodological limitation similarly applies to 
business forecasting contexts like coffee shop sales, where 
conventional time series models (e.g., Moving Average, 
ARIMA) frequently fail to account for critical external 
variables such as weather patterns, local events, or 
promotional activities that directly impact daily customer 
footfall and product demand. Just as power systems require 
integrated approaches combining climatic data with load 
patterns, coffee shop sales forecasting would benefit from 
models that incorporate both internal sales trends and 
exogenous business environment factors 
III. METHODOLOGY  
A. Data Collecting and Processing 
The forecasting model's accuracy has a significant 
influence on the end product's performance reliability and 
design optimization efficiency in addition to directly affecting 
simulation precision[9]. The dataset used in this research was 
obtained from a coffee shop's transaction data, consisting of 
two sources: training data covering historical transactions and test data covering sales in January 2025. The data was 
imported in Excel format and processed using pandas. The 
Date  column was converted to datetime format to ensure time-
based analysis could be performed correctly. Subsequently, 
the data was filtered to only include transactions for the 
product Ice Afecto , which is one of the coffee shop's flagship 
products. Sales data confirms Ice Afecto as a flagship product, 
demonstrating the highest transaction volume among all menu 
items (Figure 3). 
After filtering, the data was grouped by date to obtain the 
total daily sales. The data was then sorted chronologically, and 
the time index was set with a daily frequency to ensure data 
continuity. If there were dates without transactions, the sales 
values were filled with zero to avoid inconsistencies in the 
time series analysis. 
B. Timeseries Forecasting Models 
To forecast daily sales, three main methods were used : 
• Moving Average (MA) 
The Moving Average method was employed as a 
baseline forecasting approach. This model calculates 
the average sales over the last 7 days to predict future 
sales. While simple and easy to implement, its 
limitation is its inability to effectively capture seasonal 
patterns and long-term trends. 
• ARIMA (Auto Regressive Integrated Moving 
Average) 
The ARIMA model was used to capture trend patterns 
and relationships within the time series data. The 
optimal ARIMA parameters were determined using 
auto rima, which automatically selects the best values 
by minimizing prediction errors. This model was 
trained on the historical data and then used to forecast 
sales during the test period. 
• SARIMA (Seasonal ARIMA) 
The SARIMA model is an extension of ARIMA that 
incorporates seasonal patterns in the data. Since coffee 
shop sales may exhibit weekly variations, the 
SARIMA model uses a seasonal component with a 7-
day period (m=7) to capture recurring patterns in daily 
sales. Similar to ARIMA, the optimal SARIMA 
parameters were selected using auto Arima before the 
model was trained and applied for forecasting. 
C. Model Evaluation and Comparison 
To assess prediction accuracy, the three models were 
compared using Mean Absolute Error (MAE) and Root Mean 
Squared Error (RMSE). The evaluation was conducted by 
calculating the prediction errors against actual sales data from 
the first 7 days of January 2025. 
D. Visualization of Forecast Results 
The forecasted results were compared with actual sales 
through graphical representations to facilitate visual analysis. 
The graphs illustrate how each model follows sales trends and 
the extent of their deviations from actual values. These 
visualizations were used to determine the most suitable model 
for implementation in the coffee shop business. 2025 International Conference on Data Science and Its Applications (ICoDSA)
346Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:47:18 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
E. Implementation for Business Decision-Making 
Based on the evaluation and visualization results, the best-
performing model can be adopted by the coffee shop to 
support inventory management and marketing strategies. 
Accurate forecasting enables management to prevent stock 
shortages or surpluses and optimize staffing levels based on 
projected daily demand. 
IV. RESULT AND DISCUSSION  
 Using a variety of data aggregations and visualizations, the 
transaction data analysis was carried out to find sales trends. 
Date columns were converted, data was grouped by month, 
and basic information, such as statistical summaries and 
missing values, was displayed once the dataset was imported 
and processed. Investors have historically found it difficult to 
predict stock values because of volatility, noisy data, and 
sudden price swings during unstable economic times. This 
topic is relevant since it is necessary for both individual 
investors and, more significantly, for institutions and 
organization[10]. 
 Histograms and box plots were used to look at sales 
distributions and find outliers, while line charts were used to 
evaluate monthly sales trends and find seasonal patterns. To 
determine how each product category and payment method 
contributed to overall sales, more research was done on them. 
Best-selling items were also determined in order to create 
more successful marketing plans. 
 
Fig. 1.  Graphic of fluctuations in one year. 
 This graphic displays the fluctuations in total sales by 
month for the year 2024. It is evident that there are significant 
variations in the penjualan pola, with the highest point being 
in Mei 2024. Penurunan penjualan yang cukup tajam is 
evident in the months of March and May, and is then followed 
by fluctuating pola until the end of the year. Mei's dramatic 
increase may indicate the presence of a certain factor, 
promotion, or, conversely, an increase in demand. Further 
analysis is necessary to understand the primary cause of this 
fluctuation. 
 
Fig. 2.  Graphic of best selling products in coffeeshop. These graphic displays ten products with the highest 
overall sales. The first feature of Ice Afecto is its extremely 
low prices when compared to other products, which indicates 
that this product has very high demand. Other products, such 
as tea, lattes, and Americanos, also have significant sales 
figures that show consumers' preferences as compared to food. 
Product reviews for foods like Nasi Hainan and Chicken 
Katsu with Rice in the table indicate that even if they are not 
very substantial, some food segments do have a role in the 
process. 
The results of the model evaluation are shown in the 
following table: 
TABLE I.  TABLE OF RESULT  
Model MAE (Mean 
Absolute Error) RMSE (Root Mean 
Squared Error) 
Moving Average 
(7 day) 264,775.51 322,972.04 
ARIMA (0,0,1) 
(0,0,1) [7] 235,034.67  302,187.45  
SARIMA (0,0,1) 
(0,0,1,7) 516,775.20 572,873.02 
 
The results of the study indicate that the ARIMA (0,0,1) 
(0,0,1)[7] model with an intercept is the best model based on 
parameter optimization. With the help of the seasonal 
component (period in 7 day), this model stabilizes the 
autoregressive pattern. According to an evaluation conducted 
on January 7, 2025, the ARIMA model has better performance 
when compared to the Moving Average and SARIMA 
models, with an MAE of 235,034.67 and an RMSE of 
302,187.45. With a larger error (MAE: 264,775.51, RMSE: 
322,972.04), the Model Moving Average indicates that the 7-
day rata-rata approach is not very effective at reducing data 
variability. Conversely, the SARIMA model, which is 
obviously better in estimating seasonal, just indicates the 
worst performance (MAE: 516,775.20, RMSE: 572,873.02). 
This suggests that the musical elements included in the 
SARIMA model may not be entirely appropriate or that the 
study period does not have strong musical characteristics. 
Accordingly, the ARIMA model is more effective than other 
models in estimating the Ice Afecto  in forecasting models. 
 
Fig. 3.  Forecasting result. 
A. Forecast Results Visualization 
The graph compares actual sales of Ice Afecto with 
forecasted results from three methods: 7-day Moving Average 
2025 International Conference on Data Science and Its Applications (ICoDSA)
347Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:47:18 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
(MA), ARIMA, and SARIMA for the first 7 days of January 
2025.   
1) Actual Sales Trend   
The Actual Sales (blue line) showed a significant increase 
from January 1–3 , peaking on January 3 before declining 
until January 5  and slightly recovering on January 6 . This 
pattern indicates sharp daily fluctuations, likely influenced by 
external factors such as promotions or sudden changes in 
customer demand.   
2)  Moving Average Performance  
The MA (orange dashed line) produced a relatively flat 
forecast, failing to capture rapid trend changes since it only 
relies on the previous 7-day average. As a result, its 
predictions were less accurate compared to ARIMA.   
3) ARIMA Performance  
The ARIMA model (green line with cross markers) 
performed better than MA, with predictions closer to actual 
trends, particularly on January 2 and 6 . While it still missed 
the sharp spike on January 3 , ARIMA overall demonstrated 
superior performance in tracking fluctuations.  
4) SARIMA Performance 
The SARIMA model has been widely employed recently 
because to its increased versatility[11]. The SARIMA model 
(red line with square markers) significantly underestimated 
actual sales throughout the period. Its failure to align with 
historical seasonal patterns aligns with earlier evaluation 
results, where SARIMA recorded the highest MAE and 
RMSE among all models.   
B. Business Implications for the Coffee Shop 
Currently, almost all information is conveyed and stored 
in information system technology, such as menu information, 
discount information, and transaction data  [12]. In Afecto 
Coffee, we implement it in:  
1) Optimal Forecasting Model Selection  
For daily operations, the coffee shop’s management can 
adopt ARIMA to forecast demand for high-volume products 
like Ice Afecto . For example: The purchasing team can run 
ARIMA  forecasts weekly to estimate raw material needs 
(milk, coffee beans, syrup), minimizing overstocking or 
shortages   
2)  Limitations of Seasonal Models (SARIMA) 
Since SARIMA underperformed in predicting weekly 
patterns, the operations team must account for external 
factors:   
• Daily promotions (e.g., discounts, events)   
• Peak demand periods (e.g., weekends, collaborations, 
or community events)   
• Real-time adjustments to inventory based on observed 
trends   
3) Inventory & Stock Management 
An essential component of overall corporate policy is 
inventory management. Its primary objective is to reduce the 
expenses associated with the delivery, storage, and 
transportation of the resources utilized in the company's 
product manufacturing[13]. By leveraging ARIMA's accurate 
demand predictions, the coffee shop can significantly enhance 
its inventory management practices. The forecasting model enables precise calculation of daily ingredient requirements, 
particularly for perishable items like milk that need frequent 
replenishment to maintain product freshness. For high-
variability products such as Ice Afecto, management can 
establish intelligent safety stock levels that balance the risk of 
stockouts against potential waste. This data-driven approach 
to procurement ensures optimal  inventory turnover while 
minimizing spoilage and unnecessary costs. The system 
allows for dynamic adjustment of purchase orders based on 
predicted sales patterns, creating a responsive supply chain 
that adapts to anticipated demand fluctuations. This results in 
reduced food waste, improved cost efficiency, and consistent 
product availability - all critical factors in maintaining both 
profitability and customer satisfaction in the competitive 
coffee shop market. 
4) Workforce Scheduling 
By leveraging sales trend analysis, coffee shop managers 
can optimize staff scheduling to align with fluctuating 
customer demand. The data reveals predictable patterns, such 
as increased weekend traffic, allowing for proactive 
scheduling of additional baristas during these peak periods. To 
accommodate unpredictable surges, a flexible staffing model 
incorporating part-time workers can be quickly activated. This 
strategic approach to workforce planning ensures adequate 
coverage during busy times while maintaining cost efficiency 
during slower periods by avoiding overstaffing. The result is 
a balanced operation that delivers consistent service quality 
while controlling labor expenses, ultimately enhancing both 
employee productivity and customer satisfaction. This data-
driven methodology transforms raw sales numbers into 
actionable staffing insights that benefit the entire business 
operation. 
5) Promotion & Sales Strategies 
By leveraging sales forecast data, the coffee shop can 
develop precisely timed promotional campaigns to optimize 
revenue throughout different periods. The predictive insights 
enable strategic implementation of morning combo discounts 
(e.g., 8-10 AM specials) to stimulate off-peak sales, while 
seasonal campaigns can be deployed in anticipation of 
forecasted demand slumps. For periods showing predicted 
sales spikes, limited-time specialty offers can be introduced to 
maximize revenue potential. This targeted approach to 
promotions ensures marketing resources are allocated 
efficiently, converting predictive analytics into tangible 
business results by addressing both anticipated slow periods 
and peak opportunities. The methodology creates a balanced 
promotional calendar that aligns with customer demand 
patterns while maintaining profit margins. 
 
6) External Factor Analysis 
While ARIMA provides reliable baseline forecasts, truly 
accurate demand prediction requires accounting for external 
variables like weather patterns, social media trends, and local 
events. The coffee shop should implement real-time 
monitoring systems to track these factors and gradually 
incorporate them as predictive variables in future model 
upgrades. This comprehensive approach bridges the gap 
between statistical forecasting and real-world business 
conditions, enabling more responsive inventory and staffing 
decisions when unexpected demand fluctuations occur. 
7) Continuous Improvement 2025 International Conference on Data Science and Its Applications (ICoDSA)
348Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:47:18 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
To ensure ongoing forecast accuracy, the coffee shop 
should implement a structured improvement cycle. This 
involves conducting monthly performance reviews that 
compare predicted versus actual sales figures, identifying any 
significant variances. When deviations exceed established 
thresholds, the model should be refined through advanced 
analytical techniques or by incorporating new predictive 
variables. Parallel to technical upgrades, comprehensive staff 
training programs should be implemented to cultivate data 
literacy across all operational levels, embedding a data-driven 
decision-making culture throughout the organization. This 
holistic approach maintains model relevance amidst changing 
market conditions while maximizing the business value 
derived from forecasting insights. 
V. CONCLUSION 
This study confirms ARIMA as the optimal forecasting 
model for Ice Afecto sales, delivering superior accuracy 
(MAE: 235,034.67; RMSE: 302,187.45) compared to Moving 
Average and SARIMA approaches. The model effectively 
captures daily sales patterns, enabling coffee shops to 
optimize inventory orders for perishable ingredients and 
implement dynamic staffing schedules. These operational 
improvements directly address key challenges in waste 
reduction and labor cost management. 
The research provides an actionable framework for data-
driven decision making in Indonesia's growing coffee shop 
industry. While ARIMA serves as a reliable foundation, 
managers should complement forecasts with real-time market 
observations and periodic model updates. The methodology 
balances technical feasibility with business impact, making it 
particularly valuable for small-to-medium coffee shops 
seeking to enhance operational efficiency through predictive 
analytics. Future enhancements could integrate weather and 
promotional data to further refine predictions. 
This study has several limitations that should be 
acknowledged. First, the analysis relies solely on transaction 
data from a single coffee shop, which may limit the 
generalizability of findings to other establishments with 
different operational contexts. Second, missing sales dates 
were filled with zeros without investigating potential 
underlying causes, which could introduce bias. Third, the 
models don't incorporate external factors like weather 
conditions or local events that likely influence daily sales. 
A. Open Data Statement: 
The dataset used in this research contains sensitive 
financial and operational information from a business partner 
and is subject to confidentiality agreements. Therefore, the 
raw data cannot be shared publicly.

Accurate forecasting is crucial in Vietnam's rapidly growing 
and competitive food and beverage (F&B) industry. This study 
examines both internal factors, such as pricing, promotions, 
inventory levels, advertising, and e-commerce, as well as external 
factors, including macroeconomic conditions, government 
policies, significant events, and market fluctuations, that impact 
forecast accuracy. The findings suggest that demand forecasting 
in the F&B industry is highly sensitive to changes in consumer 
behavior, digital transformation, and external shocks, including 
the COVID-19 pandemic. The interaction between e-commerce 
growth, consumer sentiment, and market fluctuations causes 
rapid demand shifts that traditional models often fail to capture. 
Additionally, holidays, promotions, and price changes can lead to 
short-term demand spikes, underscoring the need for flexible, 
event-driven forecasting methods. This study highlights that 
combining quantitative data with internal and external variables 
enhances forecasting precision. An integrated and adaptive 
approach enables businesses to make informed decisions in 
inventory management, supply chain operations, and customer 
strategies.  
Keywords: Factor, Forecasting, F&B industry, Market 
influencing, Consumer preference 
I. INTRODUCTION  
Vietnam’s food and beverage (F&B) industry plays a 
crucial role in the national economy, contributing 17.2% to the 
GDP [1]. In 2023, industry revenue increased by 10.87% to 
reach USD 23.6 billion. This growth is expected to continue, 
with Euromonitor projecting a compound annual growth rate 
(CAGR) of 10.25% between 2023 and 2027, resulting in a 
market value of $ 34.9 billion by 2027 [2]. Additionally, the 
market is forecasted to expand by 10.92% in 2024, creating 
both opportunities and intensifying competition for domestic 
and international businesses [3]. These trends underscore the 
importance of understanding how key factors influence 
market behavior, as external disruptions and evolving 
consumer preferences can significantly impact sales 
performance. 
To provide empirical evidence of these dynamics, 
infographic representations are utilized in this study to 
illustrate the relationship between influencing factors and 
market behavior in Vietnam’s F&B sector. For example, 
according to the annual report, the chart depicting Vinamilk’s 
revenue trends over recent years highlights how fluctuations 
in consumer demand, economic volatility, and supply chain 
disruptions directly affect business outcomes. Furthermore, 
comparing Vinamilk’s revenue growth with that of the overall 
F&B industry reveals the alignment of company-specific 
trends with broader market dynamics. These visual insights reinforce the credibility of the integrated approach proposed 
in this research, demonstrating its capacity to capture the 
complex and dynamic nature of sales forecasting in Vietnam’s 
F&B industry. 
Figure 1. Revenue Trends of Vinamilk and Vietnam’s 
F&B Industry (2014–2024) 
Despite this promising forecast, demand forecasting 
remains a significant challenge for the food and beverage 
(F&B) industry. Cost fluctuations, evolving consumer 
preferences, and market uncertainties make it challenging for 
businesses to manage their supply chains efficiently and 
maintain profitability. The global beverage market is 
projected to reach 320.5 billion USD by 2025 [4], but inflation 
and supply chain disruptions pose significant risks to this 
growth [5]. 
Several key factors influence the accuracy of demand 
forecasting in the F&B sector. Macroeconomic indicators 
such as GDP growth, unemployment rates, consumer 
confidence, and government policy have a direct impact on 
consumption patterns [6]. Seasonal factors also play a crucial 
role—for instance, demand for mooncakes spikes during the 
Mid-Autumn Festival [7]. Additionally, consumer preferences 
shift with the weather, favoring light foods and cold beverages 
in summer and heartier meals in winter. These fluctuations 
pose challenges in inventory management, especially for 
perishable goods. Labor demand also fluctuates, requiring 
businesses to balance workforce shortages in low-demand 
periods with increased hiring during peak seasons [8]. 
Consumer behavior changes further complicate 
forecasting. The increasing demand for organic and eco-
friendly products, particularly among younger consumers, is 
reshaping purchasing habits, with many opting to reduce their 
consumption of non-essential items, such as coffee [9]. The 
rise of digital transformation is also redefining market 
dynamics, with 70% of Vietnamese consumers having 
purchased food online, marking a trend that is expected to 
continue growing [10]. Post-pandemic concerns regarding 
health, hygiene, and food safety have become more prominent 
[11], reinforced by regulatory frameworks such as Decree

15/2018/ND-CP, which enforces food origin transpare ncy and 
safety standards [12]. 
To navigate these challenges, businesses must close ly 
integrate demand forecasting with inventory managem ent to 
maintain optimal stock levels and avoid shortages o r 
overstocking. Vietnam’s F&B market is also uniquely  
influenced by cultural and festival-driven demand s pikes, such 
as Tet and Mid-Autumn [13,14]. These short-term 
fluctuations differ from global trends, necessitati ng that 
businesses adopt precise and adaptable forecasting methods to 
capitalize on market opportunities while minimizing  risks. 
Based on this context, this study aims to identify the key 
factors influencing demand forecasting accuracy in Vietnam’s 
food and beverage (F&B) industry. By identifying ma jor 
influencing factors and evaluating their impact, th is research 
aims to provide insights for enhancing forecast rel iability, 
thereby supporting businesses in making more inform ed and 
effective supply chain decisions. 
II.  LITERATURE  REVIEW 
Accurate demand forecasting is crucial for business es, 
enabling them to make informed decisions and adapt to 
market dynamics [15]. Instead of relying solely on historical 
trends, incorporating dynamic variables into foreca sting 
models enhances precision, enabling businesses to a djust their 
strategies proactively in response to market fluctu ations. By 
integrating historical data with real-time insights , companies 
can refine forecasts, optimize resource allocation,  and mitigate 
financial risks [16]. Several key factors influence  the accuracy 
of demand forecasting in the food and beverage (F&B ) 
industry. 
1. Price  
Price is the monetary value assigned to a product, 
influenced by production costs, target market segme nts, and 
consumers' purchasing power, among other factors [1 7]. 
Economic principles suggest an inverse relationship  between 
price and demand. When the price of a commodity dec reases, 
demand generally increases, and vice versa, assumin g other 
factors remain constant [19]. In the fast-food indu stry, pricing 
plays a crucial role in driving sales performance a nd cost 
control, directly impacting business profitability and brand 
positioning [18]. 
2. E-commerce 
E-commerce refers to the buying and selling of good s and 
services over the internet, involving financial tra nsactions and 
data exchange [20]. The retail industry has increas ingly 
shifted toward online platforms, benefiting from re duced 
physical space requirements for product display and  customer 
interaction [21]. The COVID-19 pandemic accelerated  this 
trend, with research primarily focusing on online r etail 
platforms [22]. Consequently, the rise of e-commerc e 
necessitates integrating digital platform data into  demand 
forecasting models to enhance accuracy. 
3. Advertisement 
Advertising involves the strategic dissemination of  
information through paid media channels, including 
television, newspapers, radio, digital platforms, a nd 
promotional materials [23]. Each advertising medium  has 
distinct advantages and limitations, requiring busi nesses to 
evaluate their effectiveness carefully before imple mentation [24]. The primary goal of advertising is to maximiz e consumer 
reach, ensuring that marketing efforts effectively influence 
purchasing decisions [25]. 
4. Consumer Composite Sentiment Index 
Consumer psychology plays a vital role in demand 
forecasting, especially during economic downturns a nd crises. 
The Consumer Composite Sentiment Index (CCSI) measu res 
consumers’ willingness to spend and has been used t o assess 
economic conditions. For example, during the COVID- 19 
pandemic, consumer willingness to purchase in South  Korea 
dropped to levels lower than those observed during the 2008 
financial crisis [26]. Incorporating CCSI data into  forecasting 
models helps businesses predict consumer behavior d uring 
periods of economic uncertainty. 
5. Events 
Holidays and festivals have a significant impact on  
consumer demand, with variations influenced by loca tion, 
religion, demographics, and cultural practices. Ret ailers 
situated in tourist areas or near national borders experience 
heightened demand fluctuations during festive seaso ns [28]. 
Consequently, seasonal forecasting must align with the event 
calendar and emerging consumer trends to ensure acc urate 
inventory planning and management. 
6. Physical Environment 
The physical environment plays a crucial role in de mand 
forecasting across multiple industries, including e nergy, 
tourism, and supply chain management. In logistics,  adverse 
weather conditions can disrupt supply chains and al ter 
consumer purchasing behavior. To mitigate these cha llenges, 
judgmental forecasting, incorporating expert insigh ts on 
environmental impacts, is often used to refine pred ictions 
[31]. Additionally, integrating environmental data into 
hierarchical forecasting models enhances accuracy b y 
accounting for spatial and temporal variations [32] . 
7. Promotion 
Sales promotions are time-bound marketing activitie s 
designed to elicit direct consumer responses by off ering 
additional benefits [33]. These strategies are inte gral to the 
promotional mix, aiding companies in market entry a nd 
customer retention [34]. Effective sales promotions  strengthen 
customer engagement and brand loyalty. Marketing 
communication strategies, including advertising, pu blic 
relations, and direct marketing, also influence con sumer 
purchase intentions [35]. Tanure [36] categorizes p romotional 
incentives into four dimensions: (1) monetary vs. n on-
monetary benefits, (2) immediate vs. delayed incent ives, (3) 
product-related vs. price-related offers, and (4) e conomic vs. 
psychological promotions. 
8. Macroeconomic 
Macroeconomic indicators, such as inflation, have a  
significant impact on consumer purchasing power. In flation 
erodes the value of money over time, reducing the p urchasing 
power of a unit of currency [17]. As prices increas e, 
consumers may adjust their spending patterns, which  in turn 
affects demand for food and beverage (F&B) products . 
According to VP [37], inflation-induced price hikes  reduce 
real income, limiting overall consumption and econo mic 
activity. 
9. Historical sales 
Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:46:41 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
Historical sales data serves as a fundamental input  for 
demand forecasting, offering insights into past con sumer 
behavior and market trends. Proper data collection and 
preprocessing are essential to ensuring forecast ac curacy. Han 
emphasizes the importance of reliable data sources and 
preprocessing techniques, such as data cleaning and  
normalization, in building effective sales forecast ing models 
[38]. In industries such as pharmaceuticals, struct ured 
historical data plays a pivotal role in enhancing p redictive 
accuracy. 
10. Government policy 
Government policy is defined as regulations, incent ives, 
and interventions aimed at influencing the market [ 41]. The 
Vietnamese government's issuance of Decree No. 
168/2024/NĐ-CP, which enforces stricter penalties f or blood 
alcohol violations, has directly affected on-premis e beer 
consumption behavior. Since January 2025, many rest aurants 
and pubs have reported a noticeable decline in beer  sales, 
especially during evening hours. Without timely adj ustments 
to forecasting models to reflect this policy-driven  shift in 
consumer behavior, businesses risk overstocking or revenue 
loss. Therefore, government regulation should be tr eated as a 
critical exogenous variable in demand forecasting f or 
alcoholic beverages [42]. 
11. Inventory level 
Effective inventory management is vital for busines s 
sustainability and profitability. According to Masl im [43], 
maintaining optimal stock levels boosts revenue and  
consumer confidence. Stock shortages lead to lost s ales and 
dissatisfied customers, while overstocking ties up capital and 
increases storage costs. To optimize inventory, bus inesses 
must monitor demand trends and adjust stock levels 
accordingly. Kalaoglu [44] highlights that retailer s should 
analyze market fluctuations to prevent stockouts an d 
overstocking, ensuring efficient capital turnover. 
12. Market fluctuations 
Market fluctuations impact economic stability, espe cially 
in resource-dependent industries. Hevia defines a c risis as a 
GDP drop of at least 9%, with recovery marked by a 50% 
rebound [45]. South Africa’s gold industry, once a global 
leader, has experienced declining production, which  has 
negatively impacted GDP growth [46]. Accurate reven ue 
forecasting is essential for estimating mining outp ut and its 
economic impact [47]. Businesses must integrate sce nario 
planning into forecasting models to adapt to sudden  market 
shifts and ensure accuracy. 
III.  METHODOLOGY 
A.  Key factors influencing the forecast 
Forecasting is crucial in the food and beverage (F& B) 
industry, enabling businesses to optimize their sup ply chains, 
mitigate risks, and align production with consumer demand 
[48]. Accurate predictions help prevent overstockin g or 
shortages, ensuring product availability and enhanc ing 
customer satisfaction [49]. Additionally, effective  forecasting 
enables businesses to adapt to shifting consumer tr ends, 
economic fluctuations, and unforeseen disruptions, such as 
pandemics, thereby fostering long-term competitiven ess and 
sustainability. 
A range of factors influences demand forecasting in  the 
F&B industry, broadly categorized into internal and  external factors (Figure 2). Internal factors, such as prici ng, e-
commerce activity, promotions, advertising, histori cal sales, 
and inventory levels, are within a company's contro l and can 
be adjusted to meet short-term sales targets. In co ntrast, 
external factors, including macroeconomic condition s, 
consumer sentiment, government policies, market 
fluctuations, and unforeseen events, are less contr ollable but 
significantly impact demand [50]. Understanding the  
interaction between these factors is crucial for im proving 
forecast accuracy and ensuring effective decision-m aking. 
Figure 2. Conceptual framework of the forecasting m odel  
B.  Integrated approach 
According to Figure 2, the proposed integrated appr oach 
combines internal historical data (e.g., sales volu me, pricing, 
promotions) with external, real-time inputs (e.g., consumer 
sentiment indices, macroeconomic indicators, and we ather 
conditions). The integrated forecasting model is de signed to 
enhance accuracy by combining internal operational data with 
external contextual information. Traditional foreca sting 
models in the F&B industry primarily rely on histor ical sales 
data, which often fails to capture sudden market fl uctuations, 
behavioral changes, and external shocks. To overcom e these 
limitations, the proposed approach introduces a mul ti-layered 
integration logic that incorporates diverse data ty pes into a 
unified predictive framework . 
1. Internal data 
 Internal data , such as historical sales, price, inventory 
levels, promotion schedules, and e-commerce perform ance 
metrics, provides the foundational structure for de mand 
forecasting. This data is structured, time-stamped,  and 
recorded in enterprise resource planning (ERP) or i nternal 
systems. These variables are used to identify seaso nal 
patterns, sales trends, and the impact of past mark eting 
interventions.  
2. External data 
 External data includes variables such as consumer 
sentiment (from social media or surveys), macroecon omic 
indicators (e.g., inflation rate, GDP growth), gove rnment 
policy changes, weather conditions, and public holi days or 
special events. These factors are more volatile and

unstructured but critically influence consumer beha vior and 
demand fluctuations.  
 To integrate these factors, an integrated approach  is 
required. The next section will propose the steps o f how to 
collect and integrate some approaches, such as EFA or 
ARIMA, to analyze the F&B sales in Vietnam.

IV.  ANALYSIS  AND  DISCUSSION 
A.  The interaction of Forecasting factors 
These factors are highly interconnected, influencin g each 
other throughout the forecasting process. To improv e 
accuracy, businesses must move beyond relying solel y on 
historical sales data and incorporate real-time mar ket insights, 
event-driven data, and qualitative inputs, such as customer 
feedback. This is particularly challenging in the F &B industry, 
where perishable goods, short product life cycles, seasonal 
demand shifts, and high price sensitivity add compl exity to 
forecasting [51]. 
1. Price vs. Consumer composite sentiment index 
In the Vietnamese F&B market, where price sensitivi ty is 
heightened by economic uncertainty, even small pric e 
adjustments can trigger disproportionate changes in  consumer 
sentiment. For instance, a 5% price increase in mil k tea led to 
a 15% decline in purchase volume during a mid-2023 
promotional campaign [52]. This subsequently signif icantly affects the accuracy of demand forecasting models. When 
prices increase, consumers are more likely to reass ess their 
needs, explore alternative options, or postpone the ir purchases 
unless the product is deemed essential. Conversely,  discount 
strategies tend to attract customers and drive shor t-term 
increases in demand. 
In the highly competitive food and beverage (F&B) 
market, where price sensitivity is increasing, even  minor price 
adjustments—whether upward or downward—can trigger 
abrupt shifts in demand. Forecasting models that re ly solely 
on historical sales are, therefore, at risk of gene rating biased 
or inaccurate predictions if they fail to incorpora te pricing 
variables and aggregate consumer sentiment indices as 
independent inputs. Ignoring this relationship and relying 
exclusively on past data prevents forecasting model s from 
accurately capturing the market’s responsiveness to  changes 
in price and sentiment. As a result, businesses may  
misestimate demand, leading to inventory shortages or 
surpluses, which directly undermines the efficiency  of supply 
chain operations. 
2. Advertisement vs. Consumer composite sentiment i ndex 
Advertising influences consumer perceptions, shapin g 
demand by increasing brand awareness and purchase i ntent 
[53]. Promotions often generate temporary sales spi kes, 
making it essential for forecasting models to facto r in 
advertising timing and intensity. If businesses fai l to account 
for promotional campaigns, they risk underestimatin g 
demand, leading to stockouts, or overestimating pos t-
campaign sales, causing excess inventory. Integrati ng 
advertising expenditure and consumer response data can 
improve forecast reliability. 
3. Events, Price, Promotion vs. Consumer composite 
sentiment index 
The interaction between special events, price adjus tments, 
and consumer sentiment is especially critical in Vi etnam’s 
event-driven consumption cycles. For instance, duri ng 
International Women’s Day (March 8), ShopeeFood rep orted 
a 37% spike in F&B order volume, driven by bundled 
promotions and discount codes [54]. During these pe riods, 
consumers are more likely to increase their spendin g on 
dining, gatherings, celebrations, and gifts, leadin g to a sharp, 
short-term surge in demand for food and beverage (F &B) 
products. 
To capitalize on these opportunities, F&B businesse s 
frequently launch promotional campaigns, offer seas onal 
combos, or introduce limited-edition products tailo red to 
specific events. Such marketing efforts not only at tract target 
segments such as female customers during Women’s Da y but 
also significantly boost overall consumer spending.  
According to [54], on March 8, many companies offer ed 
promotions, including complimentary gifts and disco unts of 
up to 50%, resulting in a noticeable increase in cu stomer 
traffic and requiring staff to operate at full capa city to meet 
the demand. If businesses fail to accurately encode  these 
events into their forecasting data, models may misi nterpret 
demand trends. This could lead to underestimation d uring 
high-demand periods, resulting in stockouts, or 
overestimation once the event has passed, causing e xcess 
inventory. Accurately incorporating these time-sens itive 
factors is, therefore, essential for improving fore cast reliability 
and operational efficiency.

4. Government policy vs. Macroeconomic 
Government policies, along with macroeconomic 
indicators such as inflation, GDP growth, unemploym ent rate, 
per capita income, or minimum wage, play a crucial role in 
shaping the operating environment of the food and b everage 
(F&B) industry. These factors, although indirect, h ave a 
profound impact on consumer behavior and the abilit y of 
businesses to operate and set prices, thereby clear ly 
influencing the outcomes of demand forecasting in t he food 
and beverage (F&B) sector.  
When the government introduces new regulations on f ood 
safety, traceability, or tightens input quality con trols, 
businesses are required to adjust their supply chai ns, often 
resulting in increased operational costs. These cha nges can 
affect pricing strategies and alter consumers’ will ingness to 
pay. Moreover, shifts in real income due to macroec onomic 
fluctuations or regulatory policies, such as increa ses in fuel 
prices, electricity tariffs, or value-added tax (VA T), can 
directly influence how frequently and how much cons umers 
spend on F&B products. During periods of economic 
instability or high inflation, consumers tend to sh ift toward 
lower-priced alternatives or reduce the frequency o f dining 
out. Such changes reflect a defensive demand patter n that 
cannot be fully captured by endogenous forecasting models 
relying solely on historical sales data. Therefore,  in a market 
environment characterized by volatility due to poli cy and 
macroeconomic influences, forecasting models need t o be 
“extended” to not only learn from past data but als o to 
“interpret” and incorporate real-world economic dyn amics. 
A typical example is when the government imposes or  
raises excise taxes on sugary beverages to curb con sumption 
and promote public health. This policy leads to hig her product 
prices, which in turn influence consumer behavior a nd can 
result in a decrease in demand for sugary drinks. I n response, 
F&B businesses must adjust their revenue and volume  
forecasting models accordingly and consider develop ing 
alternative products, such as sugar-free or low-sug ar 
beverages, to meet emerging market demands [51]. 
5. Inventory level, E-commerce vs. Promotion 
The relationship between inventory management and e -
commerce in the food and beverage industry is chara cterized 
by their mutual dependence on ensuring customer sat isfaction 
and optimizing operational efficiency. As F&B busin esses 
expand their online sales channels, such as food de livery apps, 
websites, or e-commerce platforms, the pressure to maintain 
accurate, flexible, and synchronized inventory beco mes more 
critical than ever. 
From a forecasting perspective, the rise in online orders 
introduces greater volatility compared to tradition al sales 
channels, especially for trending products or durin g flash sales 
campaigns. Conventional forecasting models often st ruggle to 
keep pace without incorporating input variables tha t reflect the 
frequency of online orders, user engagement on digi tal 
platforms, or product impressions over time. To enh ance 
inventory forecasting accuracy, businesses should i ntegrate 
order data from e-commerce channels into their fore casting 
models, enabling them to adjust procurement plans i n real-
time and respond promptly to fluctuating market dem and. 
The online food delivery market in Vietnam reached a 
value of VND 52.4 trillion in 2023, marking a 20.18 % 
increase compared to 2022 and a 3.5-fold rise from the pre-pandemic period. This growth reflects the increasin g 
popularity of online food ordering among Vietnamese  
consumers, driven by its convenience and ease of ac cess. It 
also represents fertile ground that F&B businesses can 
effectively tap into alongside traditional on-site sales. Among 
F&B businesses using online food delivery platforms , 47.9% 
reported that online sales account for 25–50% of th eir total 
revenue. [55]. Beyond its growing scale, the market  also 
demonstrates stable consumer habits and clear compe tition 
among food delivery platforms. The frequency of onl ine food 
orders among Vietnamese consumers remains high, wit h 
29.4% placing orders once or twice a week and 20% o rdering 
three to four times a week. In terms of market shar e, 2023 
saw significant expansion by ShopeeFood, which beca me the 
most-used platform among F&B businesses at 42.94%, 
surpassing GrabFood by 2.33% [55]. The strong growt h in 
online food delivery revenue underscores the increa sing 
importance of this channel within the modern food a nd 
beverage (F&B) industry landscape. Therefore, accur ately 
forecasting demand across e-commerce channels is es sential 
for optimizing production and distribution, maintai ning 
adequate inventory levels, ensuring timely deliveri es, and 
preventing stockouts. 
6. Physical environment, Consumer composite sentime nt 
index vs. E-commerce 
The physical environment is one of the external fac tors 
that can significantly and directly influence the c onsumer 
sentiment index in the food and beverage (F&B) indu stry. 
Unlike internal factors such as pricing or promotio nal 
programs, which businesses can control, the physica l 
environment is uncertain but can still be anticipat ed through 
meteorological data. This offers a significant adva ntage in 
improving forecast accuracy. For example, on hot da ys, the 
demand for cooling beverages such as milk tea, smoo thies, 
and fruit juices tends to rise sharply, while deman d for hot 
dishes typically decreases. In contrast, when the w eather turns 
cold or during extended periods of rain, consumers often 
prefer hot food and drinks. They are less likely to  go out, 
resulting in a higher number of delivery orders [56 ]. These 
changes can occur daily or even hourly, and if fore casting 
models fail to reflect the influence of the physica l 
environment, the results can be significantly inacc urate. 
In addition to affecting total demand, the physical  
environment also influences the form of purchase, w hether it 
is direct or through delivery. On sunny or rainy da ys, 
consumers tend to stay indoors and place more order s through 
e-commerce platforms. This means F&B businesses mus t be 
able to accurately forecast not only the types of p roducts that 
will be in demand but also the expected share of on line orders 
based on actual weather conditions. 
Therefore, it is necessary to integrate the method in 
forecasting Vietnam’s F&B industry, which has the 
interrelationship between internal and external var iables, each 
interacting in nonlinear ways. Relying solely on hi storical 
sales is inadequate in such a dynamic perspective. This paper 
highlights the need for a forecasting approach that  integrates 
structured (e.g., price and inventory level) and un structured 
(e.g., event, promotion, and sentiment) data.

B.  Example of revenue forecasting 
 
Figure 3. Forecasted vs. Actual Revenue of Vinamilk  
Under Normal and COVID-19 Scenarios (2010–2024) 
Figure 3 illustrates the revenue trends of Vinamilk   from 
2010 to 2024 under three scenarios: the company’s f orecast, 
an unexpected event scenario (COVID-19), and the ac tual 
revenue. The chart shows that during the period 202 0–2022, 
the company’s forecast slightly overestimated reven ue 
because it did not account for external shocks such  as the 
COVID-19 pandemic. 
The model integrated COVID-19 factor enables a more  
accurate representation of the decline in consumer demand 
and supply chain disruptions during the pandemic pe riod. The 
adjusted scenario aligns closely with the actual re venue data, 
effectively capturing both the downturn during the pandemic 
and the subsequent gradual recovery.  
This example highlights the importance of adopting an 
integrated forecasting approach that incorporates b oth internal 
and external factors. External shocks, such as COVI D-19, 
significantly enhances the model’s predictive capab ility, 
enabling businesses to align their strategies more effectively 
with real-world market dynamics. In contrast, relyi ng solely 
on historical sales trends without considering exte rnal 
influences can lead to substantial forecasting erro rs and 
operational inefficiencies.  
The factors identified in this study can serve as a  base 
dataset for evaluating key variables that influence  revenue 
forecasts. Furthermore, the proposed method can be utilized 
as a practical approach to improve forecasting accu racy.  
V.  CONCLUSIONS   
The F&B industry operates in a highly dynamic 
environment where multiple internal and external fa ctors 
interact to shape demand patterns. Internal drivers , such as 
pricing, promotions, advertising, inventory managem ent, and 
e-commerce performance, combined with external infl uences 
including macroeconomic conditions, government poli cies, 
consumer sentiment, and disruptive events like the COVID-
19 pandemic. These interactions create dynamic fore casting 
which can reflect real-world market behavior. 
This study presents the critical importance of inte grating 
both internal and external factors into revenue for ecasting 
models, particularly in the context of disruptive e vents such as 
the COVID-19 pandemic. The findings demonstrate tha t 
reliance on historical data alone is insufficient f or effective 
forecasting in dynamic environments. The proposed 
integrated approach not only enhances predictive ac curacy but 
also provides a foundation for strategic decision-m aking. The 
identified factors can serve as a basis for further  analysis and supporting businesses in developing more resilient and 
responsive forecasting systems. 
The proposed forecasting method remains under 
development, with further analyses, such as Explora tory 
Factor Analysis (EFA) and ARIMA modeling, required to 
identify and validate the most significant factors affecting 
F&B revenue in Vietnam. Nonetheless, this study ser ves as a 
foundational step by outlining key internal and ext ernal 
variables that potentially impact revenue forecasts . The 
integrated approach proposed here can also be appli ed in 
future studies to refine factor selection and enhan ce model 
accuracy. 
In conclusion, F&B companies are encouraged to shif t 
from sales-driven forecasting systems to adaptive, data-driven 
models that integrate both internal and external fa ctors. The 
integrated systems enable businesses to navigate un certainty, 
maintain competitiveness, and build operational res ilience in 
an increasingly volatile and fast-changing marketpl ace.
