VII. ARCHITECTURE  
According to the proposed system, the prediction of future 
sales can be achieved by the integration of many data sources, 
data preprocessing, and the application of machine learning 
techniques. This is made up of several levels: API and 
database crawling for data gathering, data preparation for 
removing noise and formatting the data and, finally, model 
development to look for cycles and trends. The trained model 
uses the received elements, including f uel prices and sales in 
the past, for making the forecast s. The last one is a layer of 
deployment that provides the simple and functional user 
interface for making real time predictions and feedback loop 
for the model improvement. Architecture like this one offers 
the precision, growth, and quantitative decision -making that 
is req uired . 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 VIII. SCOPE OF FUTURE STUDY  
Future studies on sales prediction would look into integrating 
other external variables such as geopolitical events and social 
media trends, in order to enhance the accuracy of the 
forecasts. Hybrid models combining machine learning 
techniques, like deep learning, with traditional statistical 
models may be used to enhance performance in complex 
sales patterns. Another related research may consider the 
real-time foreca sting systems where the models 
automatically update based on the current market situation. 
The application of reinforcement learning for decision -
making in stock management can be very insightful for 
enhancing sales prediction . 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Fig. 3.  Architecture Diagram of the Model Training  
CONCLUSION  
This paper evaluat es the  essential machine learning 
algorithms Decision Trees, Linear Regression, and Ridge 
Regression highlighting their programs, strengths, and 
barriers in real -global eventualities. Decision Trees include 
interpretability for classification and regression tasks in 
addition to si mple Linear Regression but decisions trees can 
have issues like overfitting and multicollinearity. Ridge  
 
 
 
  
 
 
regression is complementary to Linear Regression through 
stemming from the same area of interest and controlling 
overfitting through regularization, which make Ridge 
Regression suited to high -dimensional data. Subsequent 
studies can pay more attention to th e improvement for these 
models, including better regularization approaches and the 
construction of new blended versions for higher accuracy and 
feasibility in the realistic processes .

Weather -Integrated Recommendations for Food
Choices with SHAP Explanation  
Nikunj Jain

Abstract— In the context of today's rapidly evolving technological
landscape and the urbanization of Tier 1 and Tier 2 cities, cloud
kitchens have emerged as pivotal players, offering quick and
convenient meals without the need for dine -in facilities. The
demand pre diction for the food has become quite tough and could
be considered as one of the significant challenges . Cloud kitchens
face challenges in managing stock due to the absence of in -house
diners, potentially resulting in wasted food. Moreover, adverse
weathe r conditions often hinder the seamless delivery of online.
To address these challenges, this project employs machine
learning techniques to predict weather patterns and correlate them
with food preferences according to that weather. The key
algorithms foun d to deal with this problem were Random Forest,
kNN, Decision tree and Naïve Bayes and Support Vector Machine.
This serves the first half of the statement and then for
recommendation a suitable system was developed. The pivotal role
of the system is to re duce the food wastages for cloud kitchens by
fostering adaptive strategies for efficient decision making. The key
inputs from the market were found to be that 30 -40% of the
predicted sales in food industry doesn’t meet due to various
reasons. The ultimate goal is to empower cloud kitchen operations,
ensuring adaptive and informed decision -making in response to
changing weather dynamics. The union of both of these are
explained using Interpretability tool SHAP for a convenient study.  
Keywords:  Cloud kitchens, Decision Tree, Random Forest,
Recommendation system, SHAP, Weather pattern  
I. INTRODUCTION  
Weather predictions serve as a critical determinant in
various sectors, playing an indispensable role in strategic
decision -making, planning, and risk management. For the
food industry, especially cloud kitchens and restaurants,
understanding and predictin g weather patterns is top.
Unforeseen weather fluctuations significantly impact
customer behavior and preferences, leading to variations in
demand and sales. Predicting the weather can help businesses
plan better for changes in customer demand and manage t heir
resources efficiently. By using advanced weather forecasts
and smart computer programs, companies can adjust their
plans based on expected weather forecasts. This helps them
avoid problems caused by sudden shifts in customer behavior
due to weather ch anges. This active approach is especially
helpful for cloud kitchens and restaurants. It allows them toorganize their work better, use their resources wisely, and
reduce food waste caused by unpredictable weather. With
technology getting better, there are  different ways to predict
the weather. The study in this article explores at various
methods to find the most accurate and efficient ones . 
 
Throwing away food isn't just about losing meals and
wasting it. It also means losing a lot of money for businesses
and causing big problems for the environment. Businesses
lose money when they can't sell the food they've prepared,
and it costs money to get rid of the extra food. Plus, making
food uses up a lot of resources like water and energy, and
when food gets wasted, it's like wasting all those resources,
too. Cloud kitchens, which are becoming more popular, have
a hard time managing how much food they need and reducing  
waste. Because they don't have physical customers coming
in, things like bad weather affecting food deliveries can make
it even harder for them. According to a report by Food and
Agriculture Organization (FAO), for each food produced, one
third of it goes  to mere waste which eventually challenges the
sustainability of the planet.  
 
When weather forecasts and food businesses work
together, it can really help tackle problems. For cloud
kitchens, using these weather predictions in their planning
can make a big difference. When they use smart systems that
learn from the weather forecasts , they can make food better  
with less waste and adjust their plans. This integration  
between weather predictions and food businesses helps these
kitchens work better, matching the changing weather and
what customers want, making things more efficient and e co-
friendl y. 
II. RELATED WORK  
In [1], researchers have explored how food supply chains
are changing. The author states that there are several digital
ways to handle the supply chains. In [2], the author has
designed a  complex relationship between food distribution
and consumption. The author’s research provided valuab le
insights for policymakers, offering empirically constructed
policy models to identify potential behavioral  consequences . 
In [3] , the proposed method has integrated harnessing

fashion recommendation using Neighbour  Page Rank

The studies suggest that there are multiple challenge s 
faced by the food industry as they generate a heavy food
wastage due to multiple circumstances. Current studies
underscore the potential of machine learning techniques such
as Random Forest, kNN, Decision Tree, Naïve Bayes, and
Support Vector Machine in a ddressing the weather
prediction. However, future research could delve deeper intothe optimization of these algorithms to enhance the predictive
accuracy and efficiency. While there is vast research in  
weather prediction the integration of it with other food
industry remains underdeveloped. The proposed system
integrated  weather prediction and food industry for reducing
the wastage and improving the business.  
III. PROPOSED METHODOLOGY  
This section presents a method that  merges advanced
machine learning techniques with comprehensive data
analysis methods to address the challenges of food wastage
reduction and demand prediction in the cloud kitchen sector.
The proposed methodology incorporates various steps of
weather predi ctions using advanced machine learning
algorithms such as Random Forest, Decision Tree, Naïve
Bayes, and SHAP. Subsequently, this weather data is
meticulously integrated with food industry trends and
consumer preferences . 
 
This innovative system leverages the power of current
weather data, machine learning, and recommendation
engines to deliver personalized meal recommendations based
on forecasted weather conditions for Weather user -supplied
information, preliminary data pro cessing, product selection,
weather forecast modeling, integration and recommendation
process mix well, enabling rational decisions to be made
based on expected weather conditions on. The generated food
recommendations are presented to the user, empowering  
them to make informed decisions about their dining choices
based on the predicted weather. The Fig. 1 explains in detail
about the workflow proposed. It depicts the step -by-step
procedure which are followed in the workflow.  
 
 
Fig.1. Workflow of proposed methodology  
A. Data Collection and Preprocessing  
The dataset utilized in this study involves  Machine
Learning  (ML)  techniques to identify the weather and then

a preprocessing step, Under the “Condition”, classifications

“Fog”, “Mostly Cloudy”. The label encode r was utilized to
encode the classes  from 0 to 4 respectively.

entry was then supplemented with consumer behavior data

comprehensive discussions and analysis. No preprocessing

Data visualization is a pivotal aspect of data analysis,
enabling individuals to comprehend complex datasets and
derive meaningful insights. It involves the graphical
representation of data to explore trends, patterns, and
relationships within the dataset.  Outliers are data points that
significantly deviate from the majority of observations,
potentially skewing statistical analyses and models.  
 
To know the dataset effectively, the data visualization
plays a pivotal role in effective study. The Fig .4 portrays the
relationship between Temperature and humidity through
scatter plot not based on Time based sequence

collection errors, anomalies within the data, or rare events
that necessitate further investigation or specialized treatment
during analysis. Upon identification, these outliers are
carefully exam ined, corrected, or addressed based on their
impact and relevance to the dataset. The outliers identified
are presented in Fig .6.

C. Machine Learning Techniques  
The study employs a two -fold approach for the problem
statement. The goal is to predict weather patterns and
recommend suitable food choices based on these predictions.  
1. Random Forest:  Random forest was chosen to
handle the complex data and to offer predictions in
classification tasks. It has been adapted to predict
weather patterns based on historical data  
2. K-Nearest Neighbors (kNN):  To utilize the
proximity of neighboring points to predict weather
conditions.  
3. Decision Tree:  The reason behind choosing this
algorithm was its interpretability and adjust data
relationship. It helps in constructing a tree to
identify weather patterns and classification  
4. Naïve Bayes:  Naïve bayes was selected to utilize the
concepts of conditional probability to create
independence among features  
5. Support Vector Machines (SVM):  This helps in
classification and handles quite complex data in
simplified manner for performing tasks  
 
Each algorithm selected went through a training phase
and validation using the dataset to know its predictive
performance. The proposed work evaluated on how accurate,
precise, and reliable these models were in guessing weather
patterns. Hereafter, the predicted weathers were then
employed to recommend appropriate food categories for the
cloud kitchens keeping aim to minimize the food wastage
occurring due to unpredictable weather patterns.  
 
D. Feature Selection  
Feature selection is one of the most important parts in
implementation of any Machine learning Applications. In the
proposed work a consideration of selection of only important
features which are necessary for the model training were
selected. Various weather related and food related data points
were considered to keep intact. These included Temperature,
Dew Point , Wind Speed , Wind G ust, Pressure. The logicbehind selection of feature was forwarded with correlation
between both the components.  
E. Interpretability Tool  
Shapely additive explanations [SHAP] has been utilized
as descriptive metric for knowing the contribution of various
features. SHAP helps in splitting up the model’s output and
providing insights. With utilization of SHAP the primary aim
is to ensure the t ransparency identify which feature has more
influential factor to utilize in decision making.  The SHAP
Values are represented in the Fig. 7 and Fig. 8 respectively.  
 
 
Fig. 7. SHAP Value on Features  
 
 
Fig.8. Mean SHAP Value on Features  
 
The individual SHAP value provides insight into how
significantly a particular feature influenced the prediction.
Each variable is arranged according to its global feature
importance, with the initial variable being the most critical
and the final one considered the least important. Essentially,
SHAP offers a dual perspective, allowing us to observe the
global contribution through fea ture importance and the local
feature contribution for every instance.  
F. Food Recommendation  
Leveraging the insights derived from weather prediction,
this system incorporates a sophisticated algorithm that
correlates weather patterns with food preferences. Based on
the predicted weather conditions, the system recommends a
curated list of food cate gories and items best suited for the
anticipated climate. The main goal of this recommendation
system is to streamline cloud kitchen operations by
dynamically adapting food offerings in response to changing
weather dynamics, thereby reducing food wastage a nd
meeting customer preferences more effectively.

and interpretation of the outcomes derived from the
implementation phase. This segment focuses on assessing the

a single algorithm’s result could lead incur loses for b usiness.
So for proposed work, a  maximum predicted value is
considered. A window is created where user can enter the
weather details and the results are displayed. The user
window allowed the user to enter the number of category
present in their restaurant. Upon the need the user can ent er
the detail and get predictions/recommendations according to
weather. The basic w ork flow in simple terms with
integration of user interactive has been portrayed in Fig . 10. 
 
 
Fig. 10. User Interactive Workflow of System

subsequent figures.

From the Fig .11 and Fig .12 the user can derive to a
conclusion where a user on entering the details can have a
prediction and conclude to be “Partly Cloudy” in this case.
The further steps include the generation of Food categories
by identifying highest number of counts of predicted value.  
Food categories do include various food varieties   across the
globe.

The results from employing various machine learning
algorithms for weather prediction and subsequent food
recommendations demonstrated promising accuracy and
efficiency. The models exhibited significant capabilities in
forecasting weather patterns, enabling  the system to make
informed and adaptive food suggestions based on prevailing
weather conditions. The removal of outliers enhanced the
models' robustness, contributing to refined predictions. The
evaluation of these algorithms revealed their diverse
strengths, with each contributing uniquely to the overall
accuracy and effectiveness of the system.  
IV. CONCLUSION  & FUTURE SCOPE  
With forecast of weather and integrating it with the food
preferences, the proposed work reduce d the food wastage and
optimize inventory management. For prediction of weather 5
different algorithms were implemented to present a
showcasing output with reduced errors and a promising
result. However, as a future scope integration of additional
meteorolo gical data and exploring ensemble  methods to
enhance prediction can be done. Also adding up the system’s
future prospect can be on elevating the accuracy and user
experience. Also adding up features like to adapt through
customer trends and integration of  other customer behavior
can enhance the recommendation part. A graphical user
interface for communication and utilization of these changes
can enhance the end user’s experience. In conclusion, while
the present study marks a significant step toward addressing
food wastage challenges in the cloud kitchen industry,
continuous research and development remain essential  to
refine and optimize the system's accuracy, responsiveness,
and applicability in real -world scenarios.

Abstract—The aim focuses on improving the business risk 
prediction using the XGBoost, LightGBM  and CatBoost  models. 
The proposed model uses machine learning techniques to 
analyze business data and identify potential risks.  Then the 
approaches to help the organization  such as improve resilience, 
reduce  disruptions and support effective  decision -making . In 
this study , Two Predictive models are evaluated  Group 1  The 
existing models are used in ANN algorithms . ANN is used as a 
baseline model but shows lower per formance compared to the 
proposed ensemble model . Group 2  The propo sed models are 
used in XGBoost , LightGBM , CatBoost algorithms, 10250 
samples are used in the models because these are high 
performance, uses a large dataset and provides cost -effective 
results . The proposed model achieved an average accuracy of 
87%, while the ANN model achieved 83% . The XGBoost, 
LightGBM, and CatBoost models provide better performance 
and improve the overall prediction process . 
Keywords —XGBoost, LightGBM, CatBoost, high 
performance, more and less data, business analytics , Risk 
prediction, previous data, achieved and improvement.  
I. INTRODUCTION  
A risk -resilient framework with the power of AI -driven 
predictive analytics will be capable of optimizing supply chain 
performance and reducing disruptions. A model with real -time 
data to create better demand forecasting and efficient resource 
allocation  [1]. AI -driven supply  chain applications are used 
for risk mitigation and the framework using AI -powered 
models is forecasting the risk prediction [2]. AI increases 
warning to detection, predictive analytics, and automatic 
response systems in helping protec t critical infrastructures  [3]. 
AI-driven analytics to allow decision making at the business 
level, predictive and then binding analytics models for 
forecasting trends, automated understanding, and making real -
time responses in the marketplace [4]. AI -driven predictive 
models to authorize  business decision -making through the 
forecasting of results, mitigating risks, and authorizing 
organizations to become pro -active rather than  re-active [5]. Existing business prediction systems fail to handle 
dynamic data and often produce inaccurate risk detection. This 
study aims to improve prediction accuracy using an ensemble 
approach by combining XGBoost, LightGBM, and CatBoost 
models. This combination im proves stability and performance 
in real -time business environments.

III. MATERIALS  AND  METHODS  
The experiment process is continued by the MCA lab at 
KSR College of Engineering. The suitable dataset was 
gathered from kaggle.com for the Business Resilience 
System. This dataset is contained by system, transaction, and 
network limit can be handled by pr edicting the business 
system for failures. The models were implemented in a Python 
environment using XGBoost, LightGBM, and CatBoost 
algorithms  [14].  
Group 1: Traditional  based ANN Algorithm methods are 
used like Business Intelligence and Rule -Based Business 
Resilience techniques but AI support. The dataset consists of 
10250  samples collected from historical and real -time 
business data sources  from past businesses . Approximately 
7,175 samples were used for training the AI -Based machine 
learning models and 3,075 samples were  used in the testing.  
The parameters like system performance reports, transaction 
outline, network level indicator and the approaches can be 
supported by  post-event analysis and t hen failure prediction 
ability [15]. Group 2: The XGBoost, LightGBM, and 
CatBoost algorithms methods are improved by business 
resilience prediction, which are based on machine learning 
techniques . It contains many  samples, 10250 are collected 
from historical and real -time sources, while working on the 
dataset.  
The hyperparameters  and machine learning models were 
tuned to improve predictive performance. XGBoost was 
configured with a learning rate of 0.1 and 100 estimators, 
while LightGBM and CatBoost were tuned for optimal 
performance . The ANN model was configured with three 
hidden layers, ReLU activation, and Adam optimizer.  
The dataset was preprocessed by handling missing values, 
encoding categorical features, and normalizing numerical 
data. The data was split into training and testing sets. The 
models were trained using optimized hyperparameters and 
evaluated using standard performance metrics.     
 
Fig. 1. Workflow of Predictive Analytics for Business Resilience Systems 
Integrating AI Systems (BRS + AIS)  
Figure 1: The business data are collected from historical 
and real -time data. Dataset Preparations are collected from the 
raw data and then clean, format and completed using AI/ML 
methods. To predict the system performance and the past 
important data are a ffected in risk, To remove unwanted data 
and detect the problems. The train data is prepared by using 
XGBoost, LightGBM, CatBoost models. The system to learn 
after the failure process in the model. The data are trained 
models to check the condition like Lo w, Medium and High, 
then classify the data. The data models are adjusted to increase 
accuracy  and then help the predictions be reliable. The system 
supports continuous business operations by enabling backup 
recovery, alert generation, and load balancing . 
IV. STATISTICAL  ANALYSIS  
SPSS is a software tool, and it is used to analyze the data, 
statistical  test, and given by the results  [16].  Dependent 
variables used in final output  are predicted in the model. The 
business can be identified by the business in risk level like 
Low, Medium, High. Independent variables t-test was used to 
evaluate the significance of the performance comparison 
models like ANN Model and the proposed models . The data 
are affected by system performance, transaction task design, 
network jam signal, security event  records , and certain 
working  allocations.  The p -value obtained is less than 0.05, 
which indicates that the results are statistically significant.

VII. DISCUSSION  
The proposed model achieved higher accuracy compared to 
the ANN model based on statistical analysis . In comparison, 
the ANN model for business  intelligence and rule -based 
resilience methods are achieved of 83.74% accuracy. The 
statistically significant are (p < 0.05), This indicates that the 
proposed method is more effective for business risk 
prediction . 
 
The Quantitative research method developed by AI -based 
Business analytics can be performed in healthcare, supply -
chain and business sectors. Using SPSS tools are used in 300 
respondents to get the dataset model  [17]. AI - driven 
automation in cloud systems, they are reliable, efficient and

adoption in Vietnamese manufacturing SMEs and that impact

managers to clear the risk management. The results are  
executed by 95% in accuracy in the business methods [19].

are improved by the risk -reduced process can achieved by

The traditional da ta processing models are executed in AI -

AI-powered intelligent automatic processes are executed,  and

with human AI performed by 95% accuracy . This paper is also

prediction and the accuracy is 96%. But the business 
prediction is fast and damaging Fastly . 
 The results clearly indicate that ensemble models provide high 
performance due to their ability to handle complex patterns in 
the data compared to traditional ANN models.  
VIII. CONCLUSION  
The proposed ensemble model demonstrates improved 
accuracy and reliability in business risk prediction. This 
approach can be applied in real -time systems to support 
decision -making and enhance business continuity . The 
proposed approaches are highly recorded in 87.57% accuracy 
and then the standard deviation is 0.3688. The ANN models 
are approached by the low records in 83.74% accuracy, then 
the standard deviation is 0.064. The proposed model can be 
effectively used for real -time business risk prediction.

FILE: A_Deep_Learning_Model_Integrating_Transformer_and_MambaBlock_Modules_with_Bi-LSTM_for_Power_Load_Forecasting_Considering_Holiday_Effects.pdf

A Deep Learning Model Integrating Transformer 
and MambaBlock Modules with Bi- LSTM for 
Power Load Forecasting Considering Holiday 
Effects 
Siyang Ma  
Sino-European School of Technology of Shanghai 
University (UTSEUS),  
Shanghai University,  
Shanghai, China  
masiyang66666@163.com  Zhaohui Ning*  
School of Mechatronic and Electrical Engineering and 
Automation,  
Shanghai University,  
Shanghai, China  
*ningzhaohui666@163.com  
 
 
Abstract —Accurate power load forecasting plays a pivotal 
role in the management and operation of power systems, 
directly contributing to grid stability and optimal resource 
utilization. This study introduces an advanced deep learning 
model that combines Transformer encoders, MambaBlock 
modules, Bidirectional Long Short -Term Memory (Bi -LSTM) 
networks, and multi -head attention mechanisms. The model is 
specifically designed to incorporate the effects o f Chinese 
holidays and compensatory workdays on power demand. By 
employing extensive feature engineering and a customized 
Weighted Mean Squared Error (Weighted MSE) loss function, 
the proposed model achieves outstanding forecasting accuracy. 
On Region A’s 2014 dataset, it delivers a mean absolute error 
(MAE) of 160.20 MW, a root mean squared error (RMSE) of 
221.75 MW, and a mean absolute percentage error (MAPE) of 
2.10%. When applied to a separate dataset from Region B, the 
model maintained strong performance, with a MAE of 190.39 
MW, RMSE of 318.44 MW, and MAPE of 3.45%. These 
results highlight the model’s effectiveness in addressing 
complex time series patterns and region -specific factors, 
providing both practical significance and a valuable 
theoretical co ntribution to the field of power load forecasting.  
Keywords— Power Load Forecasting , Deep Learning , 
Transformer , MambaBlock, Bi- LSTM , Holiday Effects    
I. INTRODUCTION  
With the continuous growth of global energy demand 
and the increasing complexity of power systems, power 
load prediction plays a key role in the planning, dispatch 
and management of power systems [1], [2]. Accurate load 
forecasting not only optimizes electrical resource allocation 
and reduces operational costs, but also improves the 
stability a nd reliability of the power grid. However, power 
loads exhibit pronounced time series characteristics and are 
influenced by weather conditions, economic activities, and 
holidays [3], [4]. Among these, the effects of holidays and 
compensatory working days o n power loads are particularly 
significant, as electricity consumption patterns during these 
special periods often differ markedly from those on regular 
days [5].  
Existing power load forecasting methods mainly include 
traditional statistical techniques (e. g., Autoregressive 
Integrated Moving Average models, ARIMA), machine 
learning approaches (e.g., Support Vector Machines, Random Forests), and recently emerging deep learning 
techniques [6]. Although deep learning methods excel at 
capturing complex non -linear relationships and handling 
large -scale data, they still face challenges when accounting 
for special factors such as holidays [7]. This paper aims to 
introduce an advanced deep learning architecture combined 
with comprehensive feature engineering to deve lop a power 
load forecasting model that effectively incorporates holiday 
effects.  
The main contributions of this paper are:  
1) Feature Engineering: Various temporal and holiday 
features designed for Chinese holidays and compensatory 
working days, including lo ad patterns before and after 
holidays.  
2) Model Architecture: Proposed a deep learning model 
that integrates Transformer encoders with MambaBlock 
modules, combined with Bidirectional Long Short -Term 
Memory (Bi -LSTM) networks and Multi -head Attention 
Mechanisms, to fully capture both global and local features 
of time series data.  
3) Loss Function: Customized a Weighted Mean 
Squared Error (Weighted MSE) loss function to focus more 
on the accuracy of predictions during critical dates such as 
holidays.  
4) Exper imental Validation: Extensive experiments on 
real-world datasets were conducted to validate the 
effectiveness and demonstrate the superior performance of 
the proposed model in power load forecasting.  
II. DATA ANALYSIS  
A. Power Load Analysis

Rainfall (mm)  Daily rainfall  
Daily Average Load (MW)  Daily average power load  
This study employs daily power load data from a

enco mpasses the following variables.  
B. Factor Influence Analysis  
Power loads are influenced by a variety of factors, 
primarily including:  
1) Weather Conditions: Temperature and humidity are 
crucial weather factors affecting power loads[8]. High or low temperatures increase the use of air conditioning or 
heating devices, thereby elevating power loads [9].  
2) Temporal Features: These include season, month, day 
of the week, etc. Electricity demand varies significantly across seasons and months, and consumpti on patterns differ 
between weekdays and weekends.  
3) Holiday Effects: Holidays and compensatory working 
days have a pronounced impact on power loads. Chinese holidays encompass traditional festivals (for example, the Spring Festival, the Mid -Autumn Festival) and working 
weekends formed through compensatory arrangements, which have substantial effects on power loads [10].  
To effectively capture these factors, this study 
incorporates comprehensive temporal and holiday features during feature engineering. Additio nally, it constructs load 
pattern features for pre- and post -holidays to reflect both 
long-term and short- term impacts on power loads.  
III. P
REDICTION ALGORITHMS RESEARCH  
A. Data Preprocessing  
Before model training, comprehensive data 
preprocessing and feature engineering were performed, 
including the following steps:  
1) Date Processing: Converted the date column in the 
raw data to datetime format and generated multiple time -
related features such as year, month, day, day of the week, and season.  
2) Holiday Features: U tilized predefined holiday and 
compensatory working day dictionaries to construct features indicating whether a day is a holiday or compensatory working day, the duration of holidays, and the type of holiday.  
3) Load Pattern Features Before and After Holidays : 
Calculated the number of days to the Spring Festival and holiday cycles to capture load variation trends before and after holidays.  
4) Lag Features and Rolling Statistics: Generated lag 
features from 1 to 21 days and rolling mean and standard deviation feat ures for 7, 14, and 30 days to enhance the 
model’s memory capabilities for time series data.  
5) Interaction Features: Constructed interaction features 
between average temperature and holidays to reflect the combined effect of temperature on holiday loads.  
6) Data Cleaning: Removed rows containing missing 
values to ensure data integrity.  
7) Feature Scaling: Applied StandardScaler for feature 
standardization to improve model training efficiency and stability.  B. Mathematical Modeling Algorithms  
(1) Model Architecture  
The proposed forecasting model integrates Transformer 
encoders, MambaBlock modules, Bidirectional Long Short -
Term Memory (Bi -LSTM) networks, and multi -head 
attention mechanisms[11]. The specific architecture is illustrated in Figure 1. 
 
Figure 1 : Model Architecture Diagram  
(2) Transformer Encoder  
The Transformer model, initially proposed by Vaswani 
et al.[12], has achieved significant success in the field of natural language processing due to its parallel computation capabilities and ability to capture long -range dependencies. 
The core component of the Transformer is the multi -head 
self-attention mechanism, defined by the following equation:  
Attention (𝑄𝑄,𝐾𝐾,𝑉𝑉)=softmax�𝑄𝑄𝐾𝐾
𝑇𝑇
�𝑑𝑑𝑘𝑘�𝑉𝑉 (1) 
Where 𝑄𝑄, 𝐾𝐾, and 𝑉𝑉 represent Query, Key, and Value 
matrices, respectively, and 𝑑𝑑𝑘𝑘 is the dimension of the keys. 
The multi- head attention mechanism captures information 
from different subspaces through multiple parallel attention heads, thereby enhancin g the model’s representational 
capacity.  
In this model, the Transformer encoder is employed to 
extract global features from the input time series, aiding the model in understanding long -term dependencies.  
(3)
 MambaBlock Module  
MambaBlock is a custom neural network module 
1780
Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:44:46 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
developed in this study, integrating convolutional layers 
with State Space Models (SSM)[13]. Convolutional layers are utilized to extract local features, while the state space model captures temporal dependencies through parameterized dynamic systems. The primary computational steps of the MambaBlock include input expansion, convolution operations, state updates, and output projection, as detailed below:  
1) Input Expansion :  
Linearly expands the input features to increase the 
model’s representational capacity.  
𝑥𝑥
expanded =𝑊𝑊expand𝑥𝑥+𝑏𝑏expand  (2) 
2) Convolution Operation :  
Applies one -dimensional convolution to extract local 
features.  
𝑥𝑥conv=Conv1D�𝑥𝑥expanded� (3) 
3) State Update :  
Updates the hidden state using the state space model[13].  
ℎ𝑡𝑡=ℎ𝑡𝑡−1+𝑢𝑢𝑡𝑡𝐵𝐵 (4) 
𝑦𝑦𝑡𝑡=ℎ𝑡𝑡𝐶𝐶𝑇𝑇+𝑢𝑢𝑡𝑡𝐷𝐷 (5) 
Where ℎ𝑡𝑡 is the hidden state at time step 𝑡𝑡, 𝑢𝑢𝑡𝑡 is the 
input, and 𝐵𝐵, 𝐶𝐶, 𝐷𝐷 are trainable parameters.  
4) Output Projection:  
Projects the output of the state space model back to the 
original dimension.  
𝑦𝑦projected =𝑊𝑊project𝑦𝑦+𝑏𝑏project  (6) 
Through these steps, the MambaBlock effectively 
captures both local and global features within the time series data[13].  
(4) Bi-LSTM and Attention Mechanism  
Bidirectional Long Short -Term Memory (Bi -LSTM) 
networks enhance the model’s ability to capture sequential information by processing the data in both forward and backward directions. The fundamental computational units of Bi -LSTM include the input gate, forget gate, and output 
gate, with the update equa tions as follows:  
𝑓𝑓
𝑡𝑡=𝜎𝜎�𝑊𝑊𝑓𝑓⋅[ℎ𝑡𝑡−1,𝑥𝑥𝑡𝑡]+𝑏𝑏𝑓𝑓� (7) 
𝑖𝑖𝑡𝑡=𝜎𝜎(𝑊𝑊𝑖𝑖⋅[ℎ𝑡𝑡−1,𝑥𝑥𝑡𝑡]+𝑏𝑏𝑖𝑖) (8) 
𝐶𝐶̃𝑡𝑡=tanh(𝑊𝑊𝐶𝐶⋅[ℎ𝑡𝑡−1,𝑥𝑥𝑡𝑡]+𝑏𝑏𝐶𝐶) (9) 𝐶𝐶𝑡𝑡=𝑓𝑓𝑡𝑡∗𝐶𝐶𝑡𝑡−1+𝑖𝑖𝑡𝑡∗𝐶𝐶̃𝑡𝑡 (10) 
𝑜𝑜𝑡𝑡=𝜎𝜎(𝑊𝑊𝑜𝑜⋅[ℎ𝑡𝑡−1,𝑥𝑥𝑡𝑡]+𝑏𝑏𝑜𝑜) (11) 
ℎ𝑡𝑡=𝑜𝑜𝑡𝑡∗tanh(𝐶𝐶𝑡𝑡) (12) 
Where 𝜎𝜎 denotes the sigmoid activation function, and ∗ 
represents element -wise multiplication [14].  
The Bi -LSTM architecture allows the model to have 
access to both past and future context, improving its ability to understand and predict complex temporal dependencies in the data[15].  
Like self -attention mechanism in Transformers, the 
multi -head attention mechanism enhances the model’s 
ability to focus on different feature subspaces. It helps the model identify and prioritize critical information when processing complex time series data.  
(5) Weighted Mean Squar ed Error Loss Function  
To place greater emphasis on the accuracy of 
predictions during critical dates such as holidays, this study customizes a Weighted Mean Squared E rror (Weighted 
MSE) loss function defined as follows:  
Loss=1
𝑁𝑁
�𝑤𝑤𝑖𝑖𝑁𝑁
𝑖𝑖=1(𝑦𝑦𝑖𝑖−𝑦𝑦 �𝑖𝑖)2 (13) 
Where 𝑤𝑤𝑖𝑖 is the sample weight, 𝑦𝑦𝑖𝑖 is the true value, and 
𝑦𝑦 �𝑖𝑖 is the predicted value. Sample weights are assigned 
based on whether the sample date is a holiday and its proximity to the holiday, aiming to improve the model’s predictive performance on cr itical dates.  
C. Data Partitioning  
The dataset is divided into training and testing sets, with 
the training set covering the years 2012 to 2013 and the testing set comprising data from 2014.

IV. LOAD FORECASTING  
Based on the aforementioned features, the deep learning 
model developed in this study was trained multiple epochs 
and achieved excellent forecasting performance in the test 
set. The specific training process and model performance are detailed below:  
A. Model Training  
1) Optimizer: Utilized the AdamW optimizer [16], 
defined  by the following equation:  
𝜃𝜃
𝑡𝑡+1=𝜃𝜃𝑡𝑡−𝜂𝜂

2) Where 𝜂𝜂 is the learning rate, 𝑚𝑚𝑡𝑡 and 𝑣𝑣𝑡𝑡 are the first 
and second moment estimates, respectively, and 𝜆𝜆 is the 
weight decay parameter.  
3) Learning Rate Scheduler: Employed a Cosine 
Annealing learning rate scheduler [17], defined as:  
𝜂𝜂𝑡𝑡=𝜂𝜂min+1

period length, and 𝜂𝜂min and 𝜂𝜂max are the minimum and 
maximum learning rates, respectively.  
5) Gradient Clipping: Applied gradient clipping to 
prevent gradient explosion and enhance training stability.  
6) Early Stopping: Implemented an early stopping 
mechanism with a patience of 10 epochs, allowing training 
to halt if the validation loss showed no improvement over 10 consecutive epochs.

model  to another Region B, power load data from 2012 to

The model architecture and training parameters are kept

These results indicate that the model maintains robust 
forecasting performance in a different geographical area. 
Al- though the error metrics are slightly higher compared to 
those obtained in Region A, the model still demonstrates 
reliable predictive capability. The increased errors in 
Region B may be attributed to factors such as greater 
variability in power load patterns, differing climatic 
conditions, or unique regional demand characteristics. The 
model’s consistent performance across both regions 
emphasizes its robust generalizability and suitability for a 
variety of power systems. The successful validation on a 
secondary region further confirms its reliability and 
adaptability, demonstrating its significant potentia l for 
practical applications in power load forecasting.  
V. CONCLUSION  
This study proposes a deep learning framework for 
power load forecasting that combines Transformer encoders, 
MambaBlock modules, and Bidirectional Long Short -Term 
Memory (Bi -LSTM) networks.  By inc orporating extensive 
feature engineering, particularly the meticulous 
consideration of holidays and compensatory workdays, the 
model demonstrates a strong ability to capture complex 
time series patterns and handle load variations during 
special date s. Experimental results show that the model 
achieves exceptional forecasting accuracy in Region A 
while maintaining robust performance in Region B. These 
findings underscore its adaptability and practicality for 
application across various regional power sy stems.

A Multi-Stage BERT-Based Cognitive Framework
for Conversational Business Intelligence in SMEs
Dr. Dipali Baviskar
Department of Computer Engineering and Technology
Dr . Vishwanath Karad MIT World Peace University
Pune, India
dipali.baviskar@mitwpu.edu.inHarsh Halwai
Department of Computer Engineering and Technology
Dr . Vishwanath Karad MIT World Peace University
Pune, India
harshhalwai05@gmail.com
Anand Patekar
Department of Computer Engineering and Technology
Dr . Vishwanath Karad MIT World Peace University
Pune, India
anandpatekar03@gmail.comAnoushka Kachewar
Department of Computer Engineering and Technology
Dr . Vishwanath Karad MIT World Peace University
Pune, India
anoushkakachewar05@gmail.com
Abstract —Social media is becoming a more important tool
for small and medium-sized businesses (SMBs) to watch com-
petitors, evaluate customer sentiment, and identify new market
opportunities. However, it is challenging to convert raw signals
into understandable, actionable insights due to the unstructured,
fast-moving nature of cross-platform data. A conversational
business intelligence layer and a multi-platform trend analyt-
ics pipeline were established in earlier work on this project.
The multi-stage family of BERT-based models that serve as
that system’s cognitive foundation is the particular subject of
this research. The approach uses Sentiment-BERT for polarity-
sensitive reﬁnement, NER-BERT for business-entity extraction,
SBERT for semantic retrieval over trend-aware embeddings, and
Intent-BERT for query categorization. Within a single pipeline,
each model maintains compatibility while addressing a different
reasoning layer . We explain how these models are modiﬁed for
SMB use scenarios, how they work together to facilitate end-
to-end conversational intelligence, and how they open up new
avenues for real-time decision support, low-resource ﬁne-tuning,
and domain adaptability. This paper focuses speciﬁcally on the
cognitive foundation of that system, presenting a uniﬁed multi-
stage architecture built on BERT-based models.
Index T erms —Business Intelligence, BERT, Intent Detection,
Named Entity Recognition, SBERT, Sentiment Analysis, Small
and Medium-Sized Businesses (SMBs), Conversational AI, Cog-
nitive Framework, Social Media Analytics.
I. I NTRODUCTION
SMBs work in an environment where social media, as
opposed to traditional channels, is increasingly used to com-
municate consumer opinions, product preferences, and com-
petitive movements. Consumers discuss items on Reddit, share
trends on Instagram, write reviews on Facebook, and discuss
prospects on LinkedIn. These signals can show early demand
patterns, discontent, or new chances for a small technology
company, bakery, salon, or boutique. However, standard dash-
boards are difﬁcult for non-technical owners to understand and
manual monitoring is unfeasible due to the volume, noise, and
variety of such content.Performance in tasks like intent detection, named entity
recognition (NER), sentiment analysis, and semantic search
has signiﬁcantly improved thanks to recent developments in
transformer-based language models, and BERT in particular
[1], [2]. Building on these developments, our larger SMB
analytics project makes social intelligence more accessible
through conversational interfaces and cross-platform trend
identiﬁcation. A family of BERT-based components is essen-
tial to that overall system; each model focuses on a different
stage of the cognitive pipeline, and when combined, they allow
the system to comprehend queries, ﬁnd pertinent evidence, and
produce results that are business-friendly.
That BERT layer is isolated and investigated in this paper.
For SMB-focused business intelligence, we offer a single,
multi-stage BERT-based cognitive framework. The framework
consists of the following: Sentiment-BERT for improving
polarity interpretations when queries are sentiment-sensitive;
SBERT for semantic retrieval from a trend-aware embedding
space that captures multi-platform discussions; NER-BERT
for identifying important business entities; and Intent-BERT
for comprehending user preferences. These elements are fed
into a large language model (LLM), which generates responses
based on the evidence that was retrieved.
This study makes three contributions. In order to provide
conversational business information in SMB settings, we ﬁrst
describe the function of four customized BERT-based models
as a coherent cognitive stack. Second, we describe how each
model is modiﬁed and combined with cross-platform trend
data, emphasizing sentiment-aware reﬁnement, entity-aware
retrieval, and intent-aware routing as crucial contextual rea-
soning facilitators. We conclude by outlining potential options,
such as domain-speciﬁc ﬁne-tuning for diverse industries,
lightweight deployment strategies appropriate for businesses
with limited resources, and expanded multimodal reasoning
that can integrate textual data with behavioral and visual social
signals.2026 IEEE International Conference for Convergence in Computing Technology (I3CTCON)

ing the framework in low-resource SMB settings, emphasizinginterpretability and decision support

II. R ELA TED WORK
A. BERT and Transformer-Based NLP
Sentence categorization, NER, and question answering are
just a few of the NLP tasks where BERT’s bidirectional trans-former architecture greatly improved the state of the art [1].With domain-speciﬁc variations and more effective pretrainingtechniques, later research expanded BERT [3], [4]. Thesemodels are now widely available for real-world systems thanksto tooling ecosystems like HuggingFace Transformers [2].These models offer solid building blocks for comprehending
unstructured content from social media and noisy, domain-speciﬁc user inquiries in the context of business analytics.
B. Semantic Retrieval and SBERT
Sentence-level retrieval necessitated further tuning, de-
spite BERT’s strong performance on token-level challenges.Sentence-BERT (SBERT) is a siamese architecture that gen-erates ﬁxed-size sentence embeddings optimized for retrievaland semantic similarity [5]. Since then, SBERT has been usedin many different applications, such as topic grouping, FAQ
retrieval, and semantic search. In our project, SBERT serves as
the semantic engine that links user inquiries to representativeposts and pertinent trend clusters from the cross-platformsocial dataset.
C. Intent Detection and NER in Dialogue Systems
NER and intent detection are essential features of contem-
porary conversational agents. Early systems used rule-based orconventional machine learning classiﬁers, which had troublewith informal language and phrase variability. On intent andNER benchmarks, transformer-based methods utilizing BERTand its derivatives have demonstrated signiﬁcant improvementsin accuracy and robustness [6], [7]. This robustness is es-pecially crucial for SMBs, where questions are frequentlyexpressed informally (e.g., ”Are people upset with my deliverycharges?”).
D. Sentiment Analysis on Social Media
Numerous studies have examined sentiment analysis on
social data, including lexicon-based, deep learning, and con-
ventional machine learning techniques [8], [9]. Sarcasm, con-
text, and domain transitions have all been better handledby transformer-based sentiment models [10]. Accurate polar-ity identiﬁcation is important for SMBs when determiningwhether new trends pose hazards or opportunities. Polarity-sensitive reﬁning of retrieved evidence is the main emphasis
of our Sentiment-BERT component.
E. SMB Analytics and Business Intelligence
Small businesses frequently lack the resources, tools, or
knowledge necessary to run extensive BI stacks, accordingto research on SME and SMB analytics [11]. According tostudies, interpretation is still a bottleneck even when datais available [12]. This encourages the development of con-versational, cognitive interfaces that can protect users fromtechnical complexity without sacriﬁcing analytical depth. Thislimitation is taken into consideration when designing ourBERT-based framework.
III. M
ETHODOLOGY OVERVIEW
The entire approach creates a multi-stage cognitive pipeline
in which the output of one layer serves as structured input for
the subsequent layer, and each BERT-based model handles adistinct subtask. The crucial phases are:
1)User Query Ingestion: Text or voice inquiries in natural
language are accepted by the system.
2
Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:36:33 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
2)Intent-BERT (Intent Classiﬁcation): Trend lookup,
explanation, comparison, risk analysis, and sentimentquery are among the intent categories into which thequery is categorized.
3)NER-BERT Entity Extraction: Products, industries,
places, and rival names are examples of domain-speciﬁcitems that are identiﬁed and linked to the query.
4)SBERT (Semantic Retrieval): Semantically related
trend clusters and posts are retrieved from the cross-platform embedding repository using the enriched query,which is converted into an embedding.
5)Sentiment-BERT Sentiment Reﬁnement: Retrieved
texts are reassessed to improve polarity and intensityfor sentiment-critical inquiries.
6)LLM Reasoning: An LLM receives the structured evi-
dence set and synthesizes a concise, business-friendlyexplanation that highlights relevant trends,sentimentshifts, and potential implications for decision-making.
While the LLM offers comprehensive synthesis and expla-
nation, this methodology enables the system to break downdifﬁcult reasoning into manageable subproblems answered byspeciﬁc BERT variants.
IV . D
A TASET PREPARA TION (SUMMARY )
The trend-aware, cross-platform corpus created at the
project’s earlier stage is the source of the dataset used in thisstudy. After gathering information from Facebook, Instagram,Reddit, LinkedIn, and Twitter, it was cleaned, standardized,given sentiment scores, and organized into topic clusters. Acalculated TrendScore was used to further assess each topic’stemporal dynamics and social relevance. The generated corpusincludes platform indications for cross-source comparison,topic-level summaries with key terms, temporal statistics con-nected to TrendScore values, and post-level text or transcriptdata enhanced with sentiment labels and engagement metrics.The dataset serves three main purposes in this paper: it pro-vides domain vocabulary and query examples for optimizingintent detection and entity extraction models like Intent-BERTand NER-BERT; it serves as a semantic index for SBERT-
based retrieval; and it provides an evidence pool for polarity
reﬁnement using Sentiment-BERT and reasoning by the LLM.
V. S
YSTEM ARCHITECTURE
A. Intent-BERT
A reﬁned BERT classiﬁer called Intent-BERT was created
to ascertain the user’s goals while posing a question. Ratherthan treating every question in the same way, it groups theminto relevant business-oriented intents like trend inquiry (e.g.,”What is trending in bakery items?”), sentiment checks (e.g.,”Are people unhappy about my prices?”), risk alerts (e.g., ”Isthere any negative buzz about my shop?”), and comparisons(e.g., ”Is budget fashion more popular than premium fashionnow?”). The system can choose the most pertinent evidencefrom social trend data, route the query through the proper rea-soning path, and determine whether the ﬁnal response shouldfocus on sentiment, trend movement, competitive comparison,Intent-BERT
Identiﬁes Query Purpose
NER-BERT
Extracts Business Entities
SBERT
Retrieves Similar Trend Evidence
Sentiment-BERT (Opt.)
Reﬁnes Polarity of Retrieved Data
Fig. 2. Functional roles of BERT variants in the cognitive stack.
or opportunity-risk interpretation by accurately determiningthe user’s purpose.
B. NER-BERT
In order to ﬁnd pertinent goods, services, domains, and con-
textual information, NER-BERT extracts business-speciﬁc en-tities from user queries. These entities could include companykinds like salons or caf ´es, specialized categories like ”cake
jars,” ”hair spa,” or ”budget clothing,” local references like citynames or phrases like ”near me,” and sometimes mentions ofcompetitors or brands. Since informal or location-dependentvocabulary is frequently used in actual SMB discussions,NER-BERT is reﬁned using domain-focused data to accuratelyidentify such expressions. In order to ensure that query repliesprecisely match the user’s business interests, the retrievedentities are subsequently utilized to ﬁlter SBERT retrievaloutputs and analyze the contextual relevance of emergingtrends.
C. SBERT Semantic Retrieval
The user query and clustered social media data are projected
into a common embedding space via the SBERT semanticretrieval layer. In order to ﬁnd nearest-neighbor embeddingsthat match pertinent posts, subject summaries, or new trendclusters in the dataset, SBERT ﬁrst creates an embeddingrepresentation for the sentence when a query is delivered.The system then uses the evidence set created from theobtained matches for logic and justiﬁcation. SBERT is able toidentify that queries like ”cheap clothing,” ”budget wear,” and”affordable fashion” pertain to the same underlying conceptbecause it captures semantic similarity rather than relying onprecise language. Because of this, the retrieval procedure isresilient to linguistic diversity that frequently occurs in actualSMB-style inquiries.
D. Sentiment-BERT (Optional)
Sentiment-BERT is used to reassess the polarity of the
retrieved texts when the identiﬁed purpose incorporates sen-
3
Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:36:33 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
timent, such as complaints, satisfaction, or concerns. This
improvement makes it easier to discern between negativegrowth, which denotes new hazards, and positive growth,which denotes new business prospects. Additionally, it en-ables the algorithm to determine whether a growing trend iscommonly praised or condemned and to display only high-impact negative postings when a user shows concern aboutcomplaints. Before the evidence is sent to the LLM for ﬁnalreasoning, Sentiment-BERT serves as an optional but essentiallayer that improves the qualitative interpretation of the data.
E. LLM Reasoning Layer
The parsed intent, extracted entities, SBERT-retrieved evi-
dence, and, if necessary, Sentiment-BERT-generated sentimentinterpretations are transmitted to the LLM reasoning layer. Itcombines this organized data into a logical, comprehensibleresponse that explains what is going on, why it matters, and
how it impacts the business environment. The LLM produces
condensed, insight-driven explanations together with usefulrecommendations rather than raw analytics. For instance, itmight conclude that rising temperatures and social recom-mendations have led to a rise in consumer interest in coldbeverages. In order to capitalize on this trend, it might suggest
emphasizing cold drinks in promotional materials.
F . Explainable Output Layer
The solution adds visual analytics like trend curves, sen-
timent distributions, keyword clouds, and platform-speciﬁc
heatmaps to textual explanations to improve trust and clarity.By exposing hidden patterns and supporting recommendations,
these visual elements assist SMB users in seeing not just theﬁnal conclusions but also the supporting data. This layer facil-itates the conﬁdent interpretation of complicated social mediainformation by non-technical users and fosters transparency
and informed decision-making.
G. V oice-Assisted Interaction Layer
Lastly, users can speak their questions and hear condensed
answers thanks to a voice-assisted interaction layer. Busy
owners who might not have the time or expertise to usecomplicated dashboards will particularly beneﬁt from this.Additionally, it allows for iterative exploration by supportingfollow-up queries like ”Show me only negative comments” or”Compare this week with last week.”
VI. E
XPERIMENTAL SETUP (BRIEF )
Since the goal of this study is to establish the cognitive
BERT framework rather than perform comprehensive bench-marking, our experimental setup is presented in brief. Theframework’s three BERT-based models Intent-BERT, NER-BERT, and Sentiment-BERT were started from publicly ac-cessible pretrained models and reﬁned using a combinationof project-speciﬁc data generated from SMB-style queriesand annotated social media samples and standard benchmarkdatasets for generic language understanding [1], [5]. Intentclassiﬁcation accuracy, entity extraction performance as deter-mined by F1 scores, the relevance of SBERT-based semantic
Fig. 3. Activity workﬂow of the BERT-driven conversational BI pipeline.
retrieval as determined by both similarity metrics and human
judgment, and the qualitative improvement Sentiment-BERT
achieved in sentiment-critical business scenarios during end-to-end query analysis were the four main pipeline-relatedaspects that were evaluated. The emphasis of this evaluation ison validating the coherence and effectiveness of the proposedcognitive architecture rather than achieving state-of-the-art
performance on individual NLP benchmarks
VII. R
ESULTS AND DISCUSSION
This section summarizes observed outcomes when inte-
grating all BERT components in the SMB conversational BIframework.

Fig. 5. Comparative accuracy of non-BERT baselines versus BERT-based

A. Intent-BERT
Even in common vocabulary, Intent-BERT is able to differ-
entiate between sentiment-focused, trend-focused, comparison,and risk-related questions. Because the algorithm is aware ofwhether to highlight sentiment, TrendScore, or cross-platformpatterns in the ﬁnal response, this improves downstream rele-vance.
B. NER-BERT
NER-BERT increases retrieval precision by consistently
extracting domain-speciﬁc items like product kinds and localcontext phrases. Semantic retrieval alone may match unrelatedbut lexically comparable items in the absence of strong NER;
entity-aware ﬁltering mitigates this.C. SBERT Semantic Retrieval
Robust matching between user queries and pertinent trend
clusters is made possible using SBERT-based embeddings.This is especially useful for SMB owners that employ creativeor non-technical language. In the majority of investigatedcircumstances, qualitative analysis reveals that SBERT moreaccurately captures genuine intent than keyword search.

Offers & DiscountsUser Query: “Are people upset about prices?”
Fig. 6. Conceptual view of SBERT embedding space showing topic clusters
and the position of a user query embedding.
D. Sentiment-BERT
Sentiment-BERT allows the system to discern between
positive and negative aspects of the same trend by reﬁningthe polarity of retrieved evidence. When responding to risk-
oriented inquiries, such whether customer unhappiness is
increasing, this is essential.
E. Combined BERT Pipeline
Several specialized BERT models can cooperate as a
layered cognitive framework, as the combined pipeline
shows. This modular architecture offers greater control,interpretability, and the ﬂexibility to change or enhanceindividual components over time as opposed to a singlemonolithic model.
Overall, the integrated pipeline reduces the cognitive burden
on SMB users by transforming complex, unstructured socialsignals into interpretable insights. By decomposing reasoningacross specialized BERT components, the system improvestransparency and relevance compared to monolithic models,
making it more suitable for real-world business decision
support.
VIII. C
ONCLUSION
A conversational business intelligence system designed for
small and medium-sized enterprises is supported by a multi-stage BERT-based cognitive framework provided in this re-search. The system can comprehend natural SMB queries, ﬁndpertinent evidence in a cross-platform social media corpus,improve sentiment interpretations, and assist an LLM in pro-ducing concise, useful responses by combining Intent-BERT,NER-BERT, SBERT semantic retrieval, and Sentiment-BERTinto a cohesive pipeline. The architecture makes use of each
5
Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:36:33 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
model’s advantages: polarity modeling for subtle interpreta-
tion, semantic embeddings for retrieval, intent classiﬁcationfor routing, and entity extraction for context.
The framework offers a number of directions for further re-
search beyond this study. These include expanded multimodalreasoning that integrates visuals, audio, and structured businessdata; lighter BERT variations for deployments with limited
resources; and domain-speciﬁc pretraining for certain sectors.
All things considered, the suggested BERT-based stack showshow contemporary NLP models may be coordinated to closethe gap between unprocessed social signals and useful, dailydecision-making for SMB owners.
