Inference Efficiency on Resource-Constrained Devices .
To further evaluate the deployment ability of the proposed
models in practical environments, we conduct a comprehensive
comparison of inference efficiency on the following four
common hardware platforms.
1) # Device 1: Intel Core i5-10210U CPU with 4 cores and
8 threads (low-power CPU).
2) # Device 2: Intel Core i9-14900KF CPU with 24 cores
and 32 threads (high-performance CPU).
3) # Device 3: NVIDIA GeForce MX330 GPU with 2 GB
VRAM (entry-level GPU).
4) # Device 4: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090 D GPU with
24 GB VRAM (high-performance GPU).
These devices cover a wide range of computational capabili-
ties, from resource-constrained platforms to high-performance
environments, allowing a comprehensive assessment of model
inference efficiency. We measure four inference efficiency met-
rics and record the results in Table V. As shown in Table V, the
models searched by M3NAS-TSF exhibit clear advantages on
CPU platforms. On # Device 1, M3NAS-TSF RMSE achieves
the fastest IT (5.00 ms) and ITS (1.50 ms) and the lowest CU
(7.20%), while M3NAS-TSF knee ranks second. On # Device
2, M3NAS-TSF knee delivers the best performance in terms of
the IT (2.57 ms), ITS (0.77 ms), and CPU load (8.56%). These
results highlight the efficiency and deployment ability of our
approach on resource-constrained or CPU-based devices.
C. Estimated Performance Reliability Analysis of Subnetworks
Since the validity of the search stage in one-shot NAS relies
on a high correlation between the super-net-derived perfor-
mance and the fully trained performance (the performance
refers to the RMSE value), we check their correlation with
(a) Bike with Ly= 96
 (b) ETTh2 with Ly= 96
Fig. 8. The Kendall tau metric τand the Spearman cor-
relation coefficient Sbetween the random weight-derived
performance/super-net-derived performance and the fully
trained performance in the univariate forecasting mode.
the Spearman correlation coefficient S, and verify the ranking
consistency with the Kendall tau metric τ. The value of S
varies between -1 and +1, where positive values imply that
two groups of variables are positively correlated, and vice
versa. The value of τalso varies between -1 and +1, where τ
close to 1 indicates a strong agreement for the correspondence
between two rankings. Moreover, the random weight-derived
performance is used for comparison.
As shown in Fig. 8, taking Bike with Ly= 96 in the
univariate forecasting mode as an example, the τandSvalues
of the MoTS-Net derived performance are much higher than
those of the random weight-derived performance. This result
means that the MoTS-Net can guarantee a certain degree of
correlation between the super-net-derived performance and the
fully trained performance. However, the τandSvalues on
ETTh2 with Ly= 96 in the univariate forecasting mode are
only slightly improved. The reason may be that MoTS-Net
works for the single-path architecture search, while ignoring
the combination of low-level features and high-level features,
thus limiting the performance improvement on all the datasets
to a certain extent.
D. Diversity Analysis of Subnetworks
To further analyse the effectiveness of M3NAS, a careful
ablation study is conducted on Bike with Ly= 96 in the
univariate forecasting mode. We replace the search algorithm
of M3NAS-TSF with NSGA-II and the genetic algorithm
(GA). The detailed explanation is given as follows.
1) NSGA-II-TSF: The search algorithm in M3NAS-TSF is
replaced with NSGA-II.
2)GARMSE -TSF: The search algorithm in M3NAS-TSF
is replaced with the GA, where the objective of the GA
is to minimize the forecasting error.
3)GAparams -TSF: The search algorithm in M3NAS-TSF
is replaced with the GA, where the objective of the GA
is to minimize the number of network parameters.
Diversity Analysis in the Objective Space . Figure 9
shows the final populations in the objective space searched
by four methods ( GAparams -TSF, GARMSE -TSF, NSGA-II-
TSF, and M3NAS-TSF) under the same experimental setups.
We find that the populations of (a) and (b) are clustered in
a small region, because they are all obtained by the single-
objective optimization algorithm, which considers only theThis article has been accepted for publication in IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation. This is the author's version which has not been fully edited and

number of parameters or the forecasting error and cannot
provide decision-makers with networks balanced between dif-
ferent objectives. Moreover, the populations of (c) and (d) are
distributed around a concave curve, whose distributions are
more diverse, so decision-makers can choose suitable networks
according to their requirements.
Diversity Analysis in the Decision Space . Figure 10 shows
the NDHs and the SE values of the final populations searched
by four methods ( GAparams -TSF, GARMSE -TSF, NSGA-II-
TSF, and M3NAS-TSF). We find that the NDHs of (a) and
(b) are obviously darker on the right, i.e., the nodes of the
networks prefer node 4. In particular, in L3, L5, L7andL9of(a), only node 4 is selected, because the stride of these layers is
1, among whose candidate nodes only node 4 has no trainable
parameters. In addition, compared with (c), which has 10
nodes whose frequency is 0, (d) has 5 nodes whose frequency
is 0, which shows that the networks in the population of (d)
contain more types of candidate nodes, and (d) obtains a more
diverse distribution in the decision space. Moreover, the SE
values of (a)-(d) are 0.32, 0.38, 0.55, and 0.72, respectively.
Their values gradually increase, indicating that node diversity
is increasing, which is consistent with the visualization results
of the NDH.
Validation of Multimodal Solutions . To verify that
M3NAS yields multimodal solutions, i.e., distinct architectures
with similar performance, we analyse two local regions of
Pareto fronts on Bike with Ly= 96 and S&P500 with
Ly= 48 in univariate forecasting, where these two local
regions are delineated by the RMSE in [167.50,168.90]and
the RMSE in [6.30,6.45], respectively. From Table VI, we
observe that multiple architectures with different paths, such
as[4,0,2,0,1,4,2,0]and[3,2,1,3,4,4,4,4], achieve near-
consistent results for both RMSE and the number of pa-
rameters. This finding confirms that the proposed M3NAS
successfully finds functionally equivalent yet architecturally
diverse networks, thereby fulfilling the goal of multimodal
searching.
E. Robustness Analysis in Fault Scenarios
We conduct supplementary experiments on PM2.5 with
Ly= 168 in the multivariate forecasting mode to evaluate the
robustness of M3NAS-TSF under two typical failure scenarios:
internal model parameter faults and input data missingness.
The experimental results are presented in Tables VII and VIII,
where the minimum and second minimum values in each
column are emphasized in bold , underlined , respectively. We
utilize the Pareto-optimal architectures searched by M3NAS-This article has been accepted for publication in IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation. This is the author's version which has not been fully edited and

TSF and refer to their weighted ensemble as M3NAS-TSF pool,
where the weights are adaptively learned via a linear layer.
Internal Model Parameter Faults . We simulate soft errors
by randomly zeroing out 1%, 5%, and 10% of the model
weights in multiple TSF models. As shown in Table VII, under
1% faults, all the models perform similarly. At higher fault
rates (5% and 10%), M3NAS-TSF pool gradually outperforms
the others, achieving the best RMSE under 10% faults. This
finding demonstrates that architectural diversity helps mitigate
the impact of model parameter-level faults through comple-
mentary model behaviours.
Input Data Missingness. We simulate sensor failures by
randomly discarding 1%, 5%, and 10% of input data with
missing values imputed via linear interpolation. The results
in Table VIII show that M3NAS-TSF pool does not outper-
form under complete or 1% missing data, as some Pareto
architectures have weaker fitting ability compared to M3NAS-
TSFRMSE , whose prediction results will slightly dilute the
overall performance. However, at missing rates of 10%,
M3NAS-TSF pool achieves the best performance. This can beattributed to the diversity of the Pareto-optimal architectures,
which enables the ensemble to capture complementary patterns
and reduce over-reliance on any single architecture, thereby
enhancing robustness to missing data.
F . Hyperparameter Sensitivity Analysis
We analyse the sensitivity of M3NAS-TSF to two key hy-
perparameters: the crowding factor ( Fc) and the total number
of network layers in MoTS-Net ( w).
Crowding factor . We vary the value of crowding factor
Fcfrom 0.1 to 0.9 on S&P500 with Ly= 48 in the
univariate forecasting mode. As shown in Fig. 11, the fore-
casting accuracy of M3NAS-TSF RMSE improves until Fc
reaches 0.4, beyond which the performance decreases. This
finding indicates that a moderate Fcbest balances architectural
diversity and solution quality. Thus, Fc= 0.4is adopted as
the default.
Total number of network layers . We evaluate the MoTS-
Net depth by varying wfrom 6 to 14, i.e., the number of
searchable layers varying from 4 to 12. The results in Fig. 12
show that the RMSE of the M3NAS-TSF knee is minimized at
w= 10 , i.e., the number of searchable layers is 8, while the
number of Flops increases monotonically. Therefore, w= 10
is selected for an optimal accuracy-efficiency trade-off.
Fig. 11. Comparison in terms of the value of crowding factor
on S&P500 with Ly= 48 in the univariate forecasting mode.

VI. C ONCLUSION
To address the practical demands of efficient and robust TSF
models, this work proposes M3NAS-TSF, a multimodal mul-
tiobjective neural architecture search framework. The frame-
work comprises a modular time series super-net MoTS-Net
and a dedicated search algorithm M3NAS that minimizes both
the forecasting error and the model size to discover Pareto-
optimal architectures. In addition, the architecture-aware nich-
ing selection strategy and the architectural diversity distance
metric in M3NAS explicitly promote architectural diversity inThis article has been accepted for publication in IEEE Transactions on Evolutionary Computation. This is the author's version which has not been fully edited and 
content may change prior to final publication. Citation information: DOI 10.1109/TEVC.2026.3658547

but republication/redistribution requires IEEE permission. the Pareto set to enhance failure resilience. Empirical results
demonstrate that the diverse networks found by M3NAS-TSF
achieve competitive forecasting accuracy with compact model
sizes, offering decision-makers multiple robust alternatives to
mitigate practical uncertainties.
Future research will explore several promising directions.
First, designing a multi-path super-net tailored for time series
data helps to mix multiscale temporal features to enhance the
forecasting capability. Second, extending the current frame-
work to dynamically adapt to concept drift scenarios such as
consumer behaviour shifts is valuable for real-world robust-
ness. Finally, investigating the integration of explainable AI
techniques into the neural architecture search process could
improve the trustworthiness of the searched architectures,
which is crucial for high-stakes applications in the real world.

State-Level Drug Retail Sales Forecasting Using 
XGBoost and Facebook Prophet
 
Mohit Kamboj 
School of Electronic Engineering and 
Computer Science, 
Queen Mary University of London 
United Kingdom 
Email: m.kamboj@se20.qmul.ac.uk  Deepti Kamboj 
METICS Solutions Ltd, London,                  
United Kingdom .                                  
Email: deepti.kamboj@meticssolutions.uk   Khadija Naveed 
Department of Artificial Intelligence, 
University of Management and 
Technology Lahore, Pakistan 
Email: khadija.naveed@umt.edu.pk  
 
Sonjoy Ranjon Das 
Dep. of Comp. Science & Eng. , 
Global Banking  School United 
Kingdom  
Email: sdas@globalbanking.ac.uk  Bilal Hassan 
Faculty of Engineering & Environment, 
Northumbria University London Campus, United 
Kingdom 
Email: b.hassan@northumbria.ac.uk  Touseef Tahir 
Department of Computing, 
University of Roehampton, London 
United Kingdom 
Email: touseef.tahir@roehampton.ac.uk  
 
Abstract — Effective and location-intelligent sales forecasting is 
a strategic requirement within the emerging context of smart 
retail. The approach proposed in this study is a hybrid forecasting 
framework based on Facebook Prophet to achieve a robust time-
series pattern decomposition with XGBoost to capture nonlinear 
effects of sales drivers on the residual. This study uses the 
Rossmann drugstore chain dataset in Germany to predict state-by-
day sales with internal factors (promotions, assortments mix, store 
format) as well as external ones (public holidays, proximity of 
competitors). Strong statistical and machine-learning baselines, 
such as naive, seasonal naive, Holt, Winters and SARIMA, and 
stand-alone Prophet and XGBoost, are benchmarked against the 
hybrid. Results indicate that the proposed algorithm can deliver 
consistent improvements across the 16 German states, cutting 
median state-wise MAPE by 12 - 18% compared to the best non-
hybrid baseline. The framework produces explainable and 
accurate forecasts because it leverages the XGBoost complex 
nonlinearities with the interpretability of Prophet. In addition to 
predictive performance, the model has region-specific 
explanations: promotional effect, holiday effect, and seasonal 
variation transpose differently across states, which can be used as 
evidenced-based directions when setting inventory, promotional 
schedules, and area network intensification. The results constitute 
a scalable statistically-oriented predictive framework in the 
context of a competitive retailing scenario, and some of its aspects 
are especially new, such as regarding the use of Prophet-XGBoosts 
hybrid in geo-disaggregated pharmaceutical pharmaceutical retail 
sales. 
Keywords——  Retail Sales Forecasting, XGBoost, 
Facebook Prophet, Time Series Prediction, Geo-Spatial 
Forecasting  
I. INTRODUCTION  
 The retail market has changed drastically over the past years 
following the emergence of e-commerce, technological 
advancement, and globalization. Such radical changes have rendered retail operations to be very complicated and they need 
precise and swift decision frameworks. Of these, accurate sales 
forecasting has come to play a strategic role in achieving market 
decisions related to marketing, finance, inventory and supply 
chain logistics. Precise and accurate forecasting is associated 
with the opportunity of overstocking or stock-out; both of 
which directly impact profitability and customer satisfaction. 
Retail forecasting process is, however, a non-trivial process due 
to the ever-responsive influences of the external factors which 
include government policies, technological changes, trends in 
consumer preferences and competitive pressure. Forecasting 
models have no assurance that they will come up with reliable 
predictions unless the models can adjust to such conditions. 
Well-established statistical predictive models including Auto-
Regressive Integrated Moving Average (ARIMA) model, 
exponential smoothing and the Holt-Winters methodology have 
heretofore offered the bedrock within the predictive modeling 
of time-series [1]-[3]. Simultaneously, by using machine 
learning, predictive models, including regression analysis and 
decision trees have been implemented to extract the past sales 
pattern to extrapolate future demand [4], [5]. More 
sophisticated methods such as clustering-based methods and 
artificial neural networks (ANMs) have also enhances the 
accuracy of forecasting [6]-[8]. The work on retail forecasting 
over the last ten years has also illustrated how forecasts are 
commonly generated at the business-wide and product level, 
information which can be used in making business-level 
strategic and operations decisions about product placements, 
pricing, purchases and inventories [9]-[11]. Regardless of these 
methodological advances, the aspect of retail, although 
dynamic, has made the forecast hard because retail is defined 
by government regulation and innovation and consumer 
preferences and the competition. Consequently, retail 
forecasting has become a very important research topic in order 
to empower companies to successfully predict demand and 
align their strategies to meet it. IEEE ICTMOD 2025 1571168443
12025 IEEE International Conference on Technology Management, Operations and Decisions (ICTMOD) | 978-1-6654-7808-3/25/$31.00 ©2025 IEEE | DOI: 10.1109/ICTMOD66732.2025.11371982
Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:46:52 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
     For this purpose, we strive to gauge the effects of both internal and 
external conditions on the sales of Rossman drug store chain in 
Germany and project how many items of each category will be sold in 
each store. The rest of this paper is set up like this: Section 2 gives an 
overview of the studies we looked at, which discuss past methods for 
retail sales forecasting and the reasons behind sales trends. Section 3 
clarifies the dataset and the ways the research is carried out 
empirically. Section 4 presents the findings and conducts analysis of 
the results obtained from data from Rossman, a German company. 
Section 5 looks at what recommendations have been made and what 
research is needed going forward. The last section delivers the final 
remarks. 
II. RELATED WORK 
A. Hybrid Forecasting in Retail and Related Domains 
Hybrid strategies that integrate statistical time-series and 
machine-learning have exhibited steady accuracy 
improvements. An ARIMA ensemble coupled with Prophet and 
a "Prophet + XGBoost" ensemble detected linear trends as well 
as seasonality and residual nonlinearity in a case study in 
Rossmann, with the lowest RMSE of all models tested [12]. A 
ProphetSVR -combo handled holiday and price impacts 
through decomposition and residual modeling and enhanced 
multivariate grocery/retail forecasts [13]. Short-term power 
systems forecasting cross-domain evidence also supports the 
usefulness of hybrids albeit in terms of a reduction of error in 
the mixing of complementary models [14]. Earlier work in retail 
applications continues to focus on aggregate or one store/one 
product forecasts rather than disaggregated chains 
geographically [15], [16]. Hybrid modeling has potential 
although little is being applied to multi-region retail chains. 
 
Fig. 1.  Schematic Representation of Literature Review 
B. Methodological Overviews 
Wider surveys and technical discussions list modeling 
decisions, data problems, and evaluation approaches that apply 
to retail forecasting [17]. 
C. Traditional Time-series Models and Multivariate Casual 
Perspectives 
Other than hybrids, classical families- 
ARIMA/AR/MA/SARIMA, exponential smoothing, 
HoltWinters- are traditional benchmarks to determine 
seasonality, serial observation trends, and abrupt demand 
variations [18]. Simultaneously, such causal and multivariate 
models common in economics include promotions, holidays, 
and special events to enhance fidelity in retail forecasts [19] 
[20]; formulations that build upon regressions express these 
exogenous drivers so explicitly [22]. Although they exist, these models have a problem with non-linear interactions and shocks 
that are abrupt characteristic of modern retailing. 
D. Advanced ML Hybrids with Residual Modeling 
Zeng et al., built upon hybridization and added Prophet as a 
residual model into the mix with Bayesian-optimized XGBoost, 
achieving large RMSE/MAPE improvements when residual 
models take advantage of altering loads-an approach that can be 
applied directly to geographically divided retail demand [23]. 
E.  Deep Learning for Complex, High-Volume Data   
Deep learning methods (LSTMs/CNNs/Transformers) have 
surpassed traditional data-mining increasingly as nonlinear, 
multi-modal tasks scale/complexity is increased and have 
performed well in text, image, and speech tasks [24]– [27]. 
Deep models, particularly those used to disaggregate by state 
are not yet fully utilized in retail. 
F. Contemporary hybrid Modeling Approaches 
Recent contributions have extended hybrids further: 
CatBoostXGBoost has categorical processing and boosted 
residual learning, to deal with abrupt demand shocks [28]; a 
TransformerProphetXGBoost combination adds long-term 
procurement and short-term nonlinear effects over space [29]. 
These hybrids achieve good performance, even though this is 
seldom assessed when applied to multi-regional pharmaceutical 
retailing. 
G. Marketing, Competition and Assortment Effects 
The accuracy of the forecasts should be based on driving 
elements of demand: in-store promotions/ displays have been 
shown to boost sales by two or more times [30]; the price and 
cannibalization have the potential to transform product-level 
demand [31]; and companies are increasingly adopting the use 
of competitor promotions/sales to enhance brand forecasts [32]. 
Choice of mix/quantities (assortment optimization) impinges on 
profitability directly, and depends on credible forecasts [33]. 
The existing studies develop few region-sensitive prediction 
models that collaboratively combine both internal (promotions, 
assortment, store format) and external (competitors, holidays) 
factors. 
   The literature continues to progress in reach here, with classical 
statistical baselines giving way to machine learning and deep learning 
hybrids across [12]-[33]. Nevertheless, a number of significant limits 
continue to exist. On the one hand, the work on forecasting at the state 
level in the system of national retail chains remains scarce [12]-[16] 
[17]-[22]. Second, few studies similarly model both internal and 
external drivers in a manner sensitive to their regional variation [23]-
[27], [30]-[33]. Third, despite the potential of hybrid approaches in 
other fields, hybrid approaches have not been sufficiently used in 
pharmaceutical retail forecasting [28], [29]. The work presented here 
fills in such gaps by fitting a Prophet + XGBoost hybrid to daily 
German drugstore chain sales at the state level, with an explicit 
inclusion of promotion, assortment, store format, holidays, and 
neighboring competitor presence, and with a per-state evaluation 
reported against robust baselines. Besides, this study explored the 
Facebook Prophet technique for the first time to forecast retail sales, 
2Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:46:52 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
which is a new approach for the industry. So far, there isn’t a study 
where sales forecasting is done for different regions of the country in 
drug retailor chain while considering factors inside and outside the 
company. The research also tries to describe the geographic issues 
involved. Thus, this paper will focus only on how well both machine 
learning techniques and time series, such as Facebook Prophet, can 
predict sales in the drug store chain in each state considering the 
internal and external influencing factors. 
III. METHODOLOGY  
A. Data Sources 
The most region-sensitive and correct sales forecasting is a 
strategic prerogative in the context of the intelligently 
developing retail. The proposed research remains a hybrid 
forecasting experiment where Facebook Prophet is combined 
with XGBoost to decompose trends and seasonality changes 
robustly and characterize the remaining nonlinear effects of 
sales regressors. Based on the Rossmann drugstore chain data 
set of Germany, we will predict daily sale of the store at the 
state level, including the effect of internal factors (promotions, 
assortment mix, store format) and external forces (public 
holidays, proximity to competitors). The article is based on the 
dataset of Rossmann Sales Prediction by Kaggle including: 
train.csv: Sales and store level information on a daily basis, 
store.csv: store features of 1,115 branches in Germany.  
All the observations contain daily sales, the store identification, 
and promotion variables, holidays and store characteristics. To 
geographically disaggregate stores it was possible to map 
STORES to the 16 German federal states in the graph using 
location identifiers. That will make state an unambiguous term 
used consistently throughout this paper, as meaning 
Bundesland-level aggregation. Notably, Rossmann dataset does 
not contain product level sales. As compared to previous 
iterations of this piece, we replaced the use of randomly 
imposed product categories to study store-level, and state-level 
daily sales, which fits directly into the contribution of the paper.  
 
Fig. 2.  Workflow Diagram Outlining the Procedural Framework 
B. Data Preparation 
Sundays and holidays: The data has also an Open flag which 
denotes whether a store was in business. We do not train or test 
our models on Sundays or holiday periods, since sales are structurally zero on those trading days (the exception is that we 
have training data available, as Open [the number of trading 
days] can be zero, e. g. you might train on the full year 2019, 
with sales during both Sundays and holidays being zero). In 
case of forecast based on the calendar level, the predicted sales 
are fixed at zero on closed days to carry uniformity. 
Datenfehlern und Outliern: Forward filling was used on 
continuous variables, mode imputation on categorical variables 
to fill in missing values. Winsorized extreme outliers (values 
>3sigma rolling mean) were used to stabilize model training. 
Feature Engineering: The features were broken down into: Int 
(Promotions): Promotions (Promo, Promo2), store type (A-D), 
assort. type (basic, extra, extended). Exogenous influences: 
public holidays (StateHoliday), school holidays, day-of-week 
and proximity of competitors. Regressors were included such 
as lagged sales, rolling means (7/14/30 days) and interaction 
terms (Promo x Holiday). Encoding: Categorical variables (the 
type of store, the type of the assortment, the state) were treated 
as follows: one-hot encoding was applied in categorical 
features, and label-encoding was used in ordinal variables not 
preceded by one-hot encoding. 
This research paper used the characteristics listed in appendix 
A to predict sales in different stores across the states and 
discover what influences those sales. Features presented in 
Appendix A are the ones listed in the Rossmann data with a 
description. These factors have been split into Internal and 
External types. Besides all the other important facts, the data 
also offers details about the kind of store. There are four kinds 
of stores in the organization and they are grouped as A, B, C, 
and D. We assume, the rationale of the classification is 
grounded on the one hand on the area of the store and on the 
other hand on its sales potential. The predicator of ‘Assortment 
type’ has the following choices; (i) Basic, (ii) Extra and (iii) 
Extended and they are coded as ‘a’, ‘b’ or ‘c’. Furthermore, we 
would like to point out that the Rossmann dataset does not hold 
details about any products. Thus, by looking at the external 
sources, we managed to find out which products and brands are 
available in Rossmann stores. These groups of products have 
been randomly arranged in the stores with the RAND function. 
The dataset for the Kaggle competition is mixed with additional 
data on the States and categories a store belongs to using the 
store IDs. After that, any missing entries, extreme values, and 
relationships between variables were managed in the dataset. 
 
Fig. 3.  Correlation amongst the independent variables 
Following the removal of variables showing a lot of 
multicollinearities, we build a feature set that forms the input 
for data analysis and we include sales as the output variable. 
After that, the data is sorted and encoded by both label encoder 
and one hot encoding. 
3Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:46:52 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
C. Techniques and Algorithms 
This research will use different data mining methods to try and 
forecast future sales. For the empirical model, the company’s 
promotional strategies, type of merchandise, and store design 
are all identified as internal independent variables, and factors 
such as market competition and public holidays are seen as 
external independent variables. In brief, the methods used are 
as listed below: 
1) Linear Regression: Linear Regression uses the least 
square method to find out the connection between a 
dependent variable and one or more independent 
variables. 
,    	₁                            (1) 
where , shows the everyday sales figures for a store. X shows 
the value of the independent variable, u represents the error 
term, β1 represents the coefficients of all the explanatory 
variables, country identity is denoted by t and the time variable 
is t. 
2) Decision Tree: This technique works well because it 
is able to deal with both types of variables. Besides, 
the approach is not influenced by the changing size of 
any variable. But, the main flaw in decision trees is 
that they can fit the training data very tightly. Dealing 
with this issue requires you to use strategies such as 
pruning and trimming to manage the size of a tree. 
3) Random Forest: Random Forest was invented to 
resolve some issues that appear in Decision trees. 
Bagging is used to build base models and then the 
group of trees calculates the mean of the results at 
their final nodes. It leaves out the less important 
information and identifies factors that help make the 
prediction more accurate. 
4) XGBoost: Gradient tree boosting is an additional 
ensemble method designed to lower the bias and 
variance in the final model. By following an iterative 
method, the weak individuals are merged into a better 
trained machine. XGBoost applies gradient boosting 
in a powerful way by using only decision trees as 
basic learners and manages their complexity by 
modifying the loss function. 
5) Facebook Prophet: A new way of analyzing time 
series data, known as Facebook Prophet, has also been 
covered in this paper. The library Prophet was created 
by the central data science unit at Facebook. Here, a 
combination model is applied, fitting trends that are 
not straight through yearly, weekly, and daily 
seasonal patterns plus effects of holidays. You get the 
best benefits if the series is clearly shaped by seasonal 
variations and you have data from several seasons. It 
was able to deal with missing data, outliers, and abrupt 
changes in trends. Regarding Facebook’s usage of 
Prophet, Taylor and Letham pointed out that, in many 
situations, its predictions are more reliable and 
accurate than those produced by other methods. The main parts used by Facebook Prophet are trend, 
seasonality, and holidays. 
      ℎ  ₜ         (2) 
As for this model, g(t) is for non-linear trend, s(t) represents 
seasonal movements, and h(t) covers holiday occurrences. The 
term eₜ means the part of the time series at time t that can’t be 
explained by other factors. 
IV. RESULTS  
A. Descriptive Analysis 
Having looked at the graphs and performed simple analysis, we 
hoped to gain some helpful information before running the 
algorithms. Figure 4 gives you the details of the sales figures 
for that period. It means that revenue often reaches its highest 
level during the last months of the year. 
 
Fig. 4.  Sales over the given period 
Figure 5 illustrates the changes in sales for weekdays in several 
stores with both Promo and Promo2, which means the 
organization carried out two promotional activities in a row. 
When there is no promotion, sales are normally maximized on 
Sundays, where Promo and Promo2 are both equal to zero. 
However, it is to note that store type C is closed on Sunday 
according to the analysis of sales by each type of store. As a 
result, sales on Sundays can be mostly linked to the other three 
categories of the store called A, B, and D. It is interesting to 
notice from Figure 6 that promotions (Promo) lead to greater 
sales on Mondays. The trend was also seen among the stores 
that launched their promotions together.  
 
Fig. 5.  Weekly Sales of Store types with and without PROMO 
Figure 6 illustrates how each store’s monthly sales changed 
when they all started with a promotion and continued without 
it. In all of A, C, and D stores, the pattern was found to be the 
same. Starting in October, the sales of all these categories 
increased thanks to the stores’ promotion efforts. There are 
higher sales at Store Type B than in the other stores. 
 
 
Fig. 6.  Store types with and without PROMO Monthly Sales

On the state level, the various assortment types revealed that the 
patterns for the three categories were the same without Promo 
involved. In fact, more people are purchasing the Extended and 
Extra plans than Basic ones. There was an increase in sales for 
both ‘Basic’ and ‘Extra’ due to promotions, yet ‘Extra’ kept its 
previous sales trend. Therefore, it appears that Rossman 
answered demand for more clothing by advertising the Basic 
and Extra types, which led to higher sales. 
 
Fig. 7.  Assortment Type Sales with and without PROMO monthly 
Analysis was conducted at the state level which brought up several 
important points. 
• There are not stores of type B in all states. What’s 
more, this form of store records the highest sales 
counts in states such as NW, BE, RP, and HH when 
compared to other retail outlets. It is obvious that the 
company should expand its network of B-type stores 
to different states. 
• The most preferred categories were products for the 
skin and body and dental and oral health products in 
most states. The legislation on the sale of the various 
forms of assortments vary according to the states 
B. Empirical Analysis : Machine Learning Algorithms 
1) At the Aggregate Level: This section presents the 
outcomes we have gathered by applying multiple 
algorithms. Table I shows the outcomes that came 
from each type of forecasting. This research uses 
three different evaluation methods called Mean

squared.

The results demonstrate that XGBoost provides the best 
accuracy in predicting sales according to the popular evaluation 
indicators R squared and RMSE. It means that the organization 
can rely on the budget for taking key decisions in strategy and 
day-to-day operations. Nonetheless, looking at the business 
side, one ought to pick that method that is accurate, 
understandable, and interpretable. Most of the predictive variables in the literature have been considered and divided into 
two types in our model: internal and external factors. As 
XGBoost outperformed other solutions, we used it to find out 
the important features in the dataset. Based on the findings, 
Promo Extended Assortment type and store type c had the 
biggest effect on the sales in the stores. This way, the business 
ought to open d stores, use Extended assortments, and start 
some early promotion plans to build sales. Fig. 9 shows the list 
of results, putting them in descending order according to their 
importance. Based on the results, it can be seen that these 
models assist in predicting sales for the future. However, unlike 
time series, traditional machine learning allowed for a better 
understanding of how key parameters impact business 
decisions. For this reason, even though many new and complex 
tools have been developed, traditional methods still guide the 
company’s strategic choices. 
 
Fig. 8. Predicted sales with XGBoost 
C. Empirical Analysis : Facebook Prophet 
Next, in this study, we sought to investigate how the effects of 
our independent factors differ across different geographical 
regions. You’ll find that there are 12 German provinces 
included in the dataset. The findings at the geographical scale 
prove that all countries gain from these activities. Also, picking 
the Extended type of assortment planning would be best for the 
company since it achieved the highest sales in several states. At 
the same time, the organization should plan carefully when 
picking the type of stores for each state. In particular, state BE, 
TH, and HH have more store type D sales, whereas SN and BY 
have more of store type C sales. During this research process, 
we employed Facebook Prophet, built by the data science team 
in 2017 and has become a key method used in time series 
analysis. When making sales predictions, Facebook Prophet 
considers the effects that holidays have. This can be quite 
helpful to corporations. Within this data, this application will be 
requiring the list of school and public holidays in Germany. 
Facebook Prophet needed time as a regressor thus we should 
ensure the data set is configured in such a way. We used three 
ways to calculate the accuracy of the daily sales forecasts made 
by the model. There are MAEs of 0.16 and 0.23 and MSEs of 
0.04 and 0.09 combined with RMSEs 0.20 and 0.29 on the 
training and testing datasets.

V. DISCUSSION AND RECOMMENDATION  
These findings indicate that the hybrid Prophet and XGBoost 
model outperforms both the classical statistics and the stand-
alone machine learning baselines, with median state-level 
MAPE decreased by 12 to i 8, and estimates both accurate and 
interpretable. The feature importance analysis reveals that 
promotions, assortment type and store format represent the key 
drivers of sales with promotions increasing their weekly sales 
by up to 8 percent in North Rhinewestphalia and only 3 percent 
in Saarland and Extended or Extra assortments consistently 
increasing their sales over Basic assortments by 5-7 percent. 
Regional variation also occurs in store formats, with type B 
stores able to achieve higher sales in the NW and BE and type 
D stores able to achieve higher sales in the TH and HH, and this 
can facilitate the location-specific expansion choices. The 
effects of holidays were modest but significant in the case of 
several states, including BY and SN, and the proper 
consideration of the number of closed days decreased the 
forecasting error by 1520 percent and verified the significance 
of the calendar-conscious planning. According to these 
findings, managers must focus promotions early in the week 
and custom-control intensity by state, expand Extended and 
Extra offerings in high demand states, prioritize the 
introduction of store formats by region, and optimize inventory 
placement to local promotional sensitivity to reduce stock-outs.  
VI. CONCLUSION 
Making accurate and efficient sales forecasts is important in the 
retail industry, as a lot of decisions are made based on them. 
Following this forecasting strategy, the company won’t have to 
pay high costs for storing unsold goods and can earn more 
money. This research examined the performance of various 
machine learning algorithms after considering various aspects 
that affect the process. The use of a recent time series technique 
i.e., Facebook Prophet for forecasting sales is investigated. It 
became clear that XGBoost did better in predicting outcomes 
compared to the other models. In the same way, Facebook 
Prophet provided good results. It is necessary to note that this 
technique solely looks at the sales increase connected to the 
regressor. The results from this project could apply to other 
industries where researchers would try out Facebook Prophet.

 TexConnect: A Cloud -Based Platform  for Inventory 
and Order Management with AI -Driven Market 
Analysis for Textile MSMEs  
 
Dr. T. Shanmugapriya  
Department of Computer Science and Business Systems  
KPR Institute of Engineering and Technology  
Coimbatore, India  
priyamoons@gmail.com  
 
Mr. Logeshwaran R  
Department of Computer Science Engineering  
KPR Institute of Engineering and Technology  
Coimbatore,  India  
logeshrajkumar2025@gmail.com   
Mr. Gokul S

gokulsaravanakumar63@gmail.com

Coimbatore, India  
dhasvanthsivakumar1976@gmail.com
Abstract —This research study proposes TexConnect, a 
bilingual (Tamil –English), cloud -based web and mobile 
application that allows MSMEs and buyers to collaborate on a 
single digital platform. TexConnect provides MSMEs with 
features such as inventory synchronization in real -time; 
workflows that  track orders and deliveries; role -based access 
control; verification by an administrator; invoices; and an AI -
powered market analysis module that provides demand and 
price information. In addition, a QR -based scanning 
application is include d to provide an electronic method for 
counting and verifying the number of fabric and/or garment 
bundles when fulfilling orders.  TexConnect's platform 
leverages React, TypeScript, Supabase and AI Services to 
create a user -friendly experience for low -end devices and semi -
digital production environments that are commonly used by 
textile MSMEs in Tiruppur. Based on field observations o f 
operator and buyer interactions with textile MSMEs, there is 
evidence of increased operational transparency, order 
accuracy, and trust in communication, all of which contribute 
to an increased willingness to adopt digital systems. This paper 
outlines the system architecture, methodology and 
implementation details as well as an assessment of the 
capability of TexConnect to facili tate the digital transformation 
of manufacturing MSMEs.  
Keywords  — Bilingual interface, cloud computing,  inventory 
management,  MSME digitization , order tracking, AI market 
analysis, QR -based scanning, textile manufacturing.  
I. INTRODUCTION  
 The textile sector is a large part of the Indian 
manufactured economy. Micro, Small and Medium sized 
Enterprises (MSMEs), which are small businesses, have a 
significant presence in production clusters such as Tiruppur. 
Many textile MSMEs still do not have a fully developed system of manual record keeping and informal methods of 
communicating with one another about their products and 
how they manage their inventory and track orders. These 
kinds of manual systems of record -keeping and 
communication create inc onsistencies with the inventory 
levels of products, delays between buyers and suppliers, and 
do not provide very much operational transparency.As there 
is a growing demand for faster order fulfillment and 
dependable buyer/supplier communication, the need f or the 
digitization of business operations in the textile 
manufacturing industries has become apparent. Current 
computer systems for textile manufacturing are generally too 
complicated or do not reflect the operational realities of 
micro, small or medium s ized enterprises. Because MSMEs 
also do not have access to real -time data about market 
conditions, their pricing and production decision making is 
based on inadequate information.  
 In response to these problems, the present 
study introduces TexConnect, a multilingual (Tamil -
English) cloud -supported web and mobile service created for 
organizations in the textile industry and their clients. This 
service provides real -time inventory man agement and order 
status tracking, role -based access control, administrative 
approval for invoice generation, and GST compliant invoice 
production. Included is an AI -enabled market analysis 
module to determine demand and pricing for the goods; also 
includ ed is a QR code -enabled implementation to use mobile 
scanning devices to verify the packages provided for 
shipment.  The cloud infrastructure utilized by the TX 
System as well as its decision support system based on 
artificial intelligence allow for increased transparency, Proceedings of the 9th International Conference on Intelligent Computing and Control Systems (ICICCS-2026)
IEEE XPlore Part Number: CFP26K74-ART; ISBN: 979-8-3315-8947-9
979-8-3315-8947-9/26/$31.00 ©2026 IEEE 19112026 9th International Conference on Intelligent Computing and Control Systems (ICICCS) | 979-8-3315-8947-9/26/$31.00 ©2026 IEEE | DOI: 10.1109/ICICCS67901.2026.11502860
Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:38:43 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
 
 improved accuracy, and increased collaboration throughout 
the textile supply chain.  
 This paper will describe the system design 
process, implementation process, evaluation of the 
TexConnect system, and the degree that this system will 
benefit textile MSMEs through their digital transformation.  
 
II. LITERATURE SURVEY  
 Digitizing processes for micro, small, and 
medium enterprises (MSMEs) has been a focus of research 
in IEEE journals. In particular, studies have shown that 
online web -based applications in the cloud have been the 
easiest and the least costly manner for sma ll business to  
change from paper to digital. For micro and small 
manufacturing, Peter Baumgartner et al. [1] showed that 
browser -based inventory applications significantly reduced 
errors in operations. Chandra and Gupta [2] also showed that 
the cloud, in a similar fashion, better facilitated access to 
data for small business, without the requisite infrastructure 
costs. As noted by Lin and Wei [3], real -time DB transitions 
are integral to TexConnect's cloud base system, which has 
been a successfully proven method that improves stock 
reporting accuracy, and reduces the incidence of stock 
mismatches.  Use of bilingual and localized user interfaces 
are also crucial in technology adoption. Prakash and Senthil 
[4] demonstrated that a multilingual user interface im proved 
usability for industrial workers that do not speak English, 
which endorses the Tamil –English interface model proposed 
for TexConnect. Lastly, in supply chain -managers of buyer 
supplier systems, Gonzalez et al. [5] concluded that a 
structured digital  web-based order -tracking system was 
successful in improving communication and decreasing 
delays. These findings strongly support the justification for 
one web application, like TexConnect, that encompasses an 
inventory system, order, and communication sys tem, for the 
textile MSME enterprise  [6], [7] .Overall, the current IEEE 
literature reaffirms that a lightweight, multilingual, cloud -
based web application is the best option for MSMEs to 
leverage as they undergo the transition to an Industry 4.0 
environment.  
 
III. SYSTEM OVERVIEW  
 TexConnect digitizes three primary user roles: 
The Members of the MSME family are able to manage their 
products, update their stock levels, track their orders, and 
create invoices. The Buyer can view all items available for 
order; place an order; and check the status of their order. The 
Administrator i s responsible for authenticating all Members 
of the MSME (who wish to sell products) and monitoring the 
overall integrity of the platform. The bilingual interface (Tamil and English) has been designed to make it easier for 
semi -digital users to use the sys tem and have the ability to 
switch back and forth between languages quickly and easily.  
 
As shown  in Fig. 1: The overall flow of TexConnect shows how MSMEs 
will interact with buyers and administrators using the digital platform.  
 
IV. SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE  
TexConnect uses a Five-layer architecture:  
A. Frontend (React + TypeScript)  
 TexConnect's frontend is built on top of a 
combination of React and TypeScript, providing a structured 
and efficient framework for the user interface layer. React 
Router provides seamless navigation throughout multiple 
modules, such as Inventory, Orders, a nd Analytics, allowing 
for instant transitions from one screen to another. The front 
end's bilingual capabilities are built on the React Context 
API, providing users the ability to easily switch back and 
forth between Tamil and English on all UI elements 
instantaneously without reloading the entire page. Tailwind 
CSS is used to produce a modern and mobile -friendly 
design, ensuring MSMEs that typically use the TexConnect 
Platform from many different devices can expect a clean and 
uniform look regardless of h ow they access it. The frontend 
also communicates directly with the backend via the 
Supabase Client, allowing for real -time data synchronization 
and secure API communications. In addition, Recharts 
generates attractive and easy -to-understand charts and 
graphs that help users better understand how their business 
is performing. Together these technologies create the 
responsive and lightweight environment that enables 
TexConnect to offer a desktop -like user experience to users 
while they are in their web brows er. 
B. Backend (Supabase Platform)  
 Supabase powers all of the back -end features 
of TexConnect; Supabase Cloud will offer a cloud -based 
fully -managed architecture that supports real -time 
applications for industrial purposes. Supabase's use of 
PostgreSQL as their database allows for structure d, scalable 
storage of user information, inventory information, order 
information, and logs. Supabase uses a JWT token -based 
authentication system, which provides role -specific access 
Proceedings of the 9th International Conference on Intelligent Computing and Control Systems (ICICCS-2026)
IEEE XPlore Part Number: CFP26K74-ART; ISBN: 979-8-3315-8947-9
979-8-3315-8947-9/26/$31.00 ©2026 IEEE 1912
Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:38:43 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
 
 to MSMEs, buyers and administrators. The Supabase real -
time engine enables real -time updates for all users - so that 
updates on order statuses, stock and notifications will show 
up instantly on all users' connected devices. The Supabase 
Row -Level Security feature allows strict access control to 
data by enabling users to view only those records of data 
they have created. All application calls to the backend utilize 
the REST API, which provides the means to perform 
standard CRUD operations (Create, Read, Upda te, Delete) 
and keep data in sync across all users and devices.  
C. Communication Layer  
 The communication layer allows for an 
efficient and secure method of moving information between 
the Front -End and Back -End. API requests and responses are 
sent via HTTPS, ensuring that user data is encrypted for 
confidentiality during transmission. Additio nally, 
TexConnect uses Realtime WebSocket listeners to provide 
immediate updates to the client -side of the application 
regarding stock alerts, order progress, and dashboard 
refreshes without requiring the user to refresh their browser. 
To further optimize  data transfer, TexConnect uses a 
lightweight format of JSON as its information exchange 
format. This helps reduce bandwidth consumption by a large 
margin, while maintaining a high level of performance in 
low-connectivity environments as typically found in  
industrial areas. Therefore, the combination of these 
efficiencies in our communication stack contribute to 
making TexConnect feel more like an application and less 
like a website when being viewed within a web browser.  
D. AI Layer  
The AI layer is an intelligent part of the 
TexConnect system that leverages historical order data, 
stock levels and production timelines to provide predictive 
analysis of potential delays as well as trends in customer 
demand. The AI layer generates proacti ve notifications for 
low stock levels and delayed orders, and interacts with other 
components of the system via well -defined APIs to provide 
real-time insight while enabling modular architecture and 
scalability of the overall system. This design allows for  
decision support from AI without interrupting the ability of 
users to use the core system.  
E. QR Code Tracking Layer  
 The Tracking Layer helps with real -time 
tracking of customer's and M -SMEs' orders. Customers will 
be able to see the status of their order in real time through a 
QR Codes uniquely assigned to that order. This Tracking 
Layer module talks to both the Front E nd and Back End of 
the system, allowing interaction with users without violating any of the users' privacy rights or security. The user receives 
fast, reliable and accessible information when tracking an 
order through this layer, which is designed to seaml essly fit 
within the overall system architecture.

(b)

(presentation, application, and data), a functional block diagram of the 
system is presented in Fig. 2(b).  
 
V. METHODOLOGY  
 TexConnect was developed using a user -
centered iterative design approach. Site visits were made to 
meet with textile MSME owners and workers in Tirupur to 
understand their requirements. Workflow wireframes were 
created based on the information collected fr om those site 
visits, which modelled how inventory updates, how to 
transition from receiving/producing an order to when 
payment is made, and how to create an invoice. As part of 
the design, a bilingual content structure supporting Tamil 
and English was cr eated. The front end of the application 
was developed using React with TypeScript and the back 
end was developed using Supabase for real -time sync and 
integration. An AI module was added to perform demand 
trend analysis using the historical transaction dat a that had 
already been recorded. The test and debug phase was 
conducted on low -end smartphones from multiple sites with 
Proceedings of the 9th International Conference on Intelligent Computing and Control Systems (ICICCS-2026)
IEEE XPlore Part Number: CFP26K74-ART; ISBN: 979-8-3315-8947-9

 varying bandwidth conditions to identify and resolve issues 
prior to launch. MSME user feedback was used to modify 
the application for ease of use, particularly around 
addressing the challenges associated with language, device 
limitations and slow Internet  connectivity.  
VI. IMPLEMENTATION  
The system comprises the following key features:  
A. Authentication and Roles : 
When a user registers with Supabase’s 
authentication service, that user is assigned one of three 
roles - Buyer, MSME, or Admin. The role assigned to each 
user determines the pages and features that user can 
access. By restricting access  to only those  features  and 
pages  that are assigned  to a user’s role, Supabase ensures 
that the functionality presented to each  user is appropriate  
for their  role.  If a user attempts  to access  a page  that they 
do not have  permission  for, they will automatically be 
redirected to the home page which maintains the security 
and integrity of the system and provides proper access 
control. This not only protects sensitive information but 
also improves the overall experience  for users;  since  only 
the features  that users  have permission to use will be 
displayed, the user will not have any difficulty 
understanding what they can use.  
 
 
As shown  in Fig. 3: The authentication module confirms the credentials of 
each user and assigns role specific access to their associated dashboard.  
B. Inventory Management  
 The inventory management module of the 
system provides MSME users with tools to manage their 
stock more effectively.  Users  can create  new items  when  
a new product arrives and adjust their current stock to 
reflect changes in quantity/price/description,  as well as 
remove  a discontinued or otherwise unavailable item. The 
system continuously updates  and reports  changes  to stock  
through  the use of real- time database listeners, which will 
provide each user with the  most  current  and accurate  data regarding  the availability of products, thereby minimizing 
error associated with outdated records, thus increasing 
overall operational efficiencies.  
 
 
As shown  in Fig. 4: The MSME inventory module updates stock 
information in real time and will generate notifications when stock levels 
drop below a certain threshold.  
C. Order Workflow  
The system monitors  every  order  through 
the steps of: Requesting, Accepting, Processing, Packing 
and Completing. For each order's progress the order status 
gets changed automatically within the system and 
available for the buyer in real -time, which gives visibility to 
the buyer into their order's progress/visibility. This level 
of transparency allows the user to keep informed on the 
order's progress, decreasing the amount of uncertainty 
and enabling businesses to effectively manage customer 
expectations. Ha ving the buyers  receive  immediate  
updates  also decreases any confusion caused by a lack of 
communication and enhance overall customer 
satisfaction.  
 
 
As shown  in Fig. 4: The MSME inventory module updates stock 
information in real time and will generate notifications when stock 
levels drop below a certain threshold.  
D. Invoice Generator  
Proceedings of the 9th International Conference on Intelligent Computing and Control Systems (ICICCS-2026)
IEEE XPlore Part Number: CFP26K74-ART; ISBN: 979-8-3315-8947-9
979-8-3315-8947-9/26/$31.00 ©2026 IEEE 1914
Authorized licensed use limited to: Mukesh Patel School of Technology & Engineering. Downloaded on July 10,2026 at 15:38:43 UTC from IEEE Xplore.  Restrictions apply. 
 
 Using an  application  called TexConnect,  
you will be able to create invoices automatically instead 
of having to create them manually. To do this, 
TexConnect captures the order information you input into 
an HTML page using a library called html2canvas and 
saves it in a PDF format using another library called 
jsPDF. An invoice includes important information: your 
invoice must contain product descriptions, the quantity 
ordered, the price of the product,  GST tax  value,  and the  
buyer's  name and any logo you would like them to see. 
With automated invoic e creation,  data entry  errors  are 
reduced  and time spent  on this task is reduced while 
ensuring the generated invoice will remain  consistent  and 
look professional.  When  your  invoice is  generated,  it is 
stored  securely  in the  cloud,  allowing  you (as an MSME) 
to refer to them at any time for reference, reporting or 
audits. To summarize, the module improves your billing 
processes and increases productivity within your small 
business economy.  
E. Bilingual Engine  
The application's text elements (labels, 
buttons, notifications, messages) come from a central 
language dictionary and have a unique identifier (key). 
All text has been replaced with a corresponding entry in 
the language  dictionary,  rather  than being  incorporated  
directly into the application user interface (UI). When an 
end user changes  the language  of the application,  all 
instances  of text displayed in the application will 
automatically change to reflect the selected language 
without requiring them to refresh the webpage. Therefore, 
it allows for a consistent, seamless  experience for  users 
with multiple languages, and by allowing for easy 
addition and/or modification of a language  by only 
updating  entries  in the language  dictionary.  
F. Analytics Dashboard  
TexConnect  has Official Analytics Dashboard 
for MSMEs that provides a clear view of the operation of 
the company. It provides the business owner with a visually 
representative graph for Sales Trends, current stock levels/ 
current order volume, etc., using Recharts o ver time. By 
using Recharts, the Business owner can quickly identify 
trends, recognize possible shortages, make decisions for 
Inventory & Production based on solid data via the graphical 
charts that have been constructed. Creating charts with data 
that can  be visually seen in a simple graphical format helps 
to reduce errors, save time, and improve overall operational 
efficiency. Through these Graphical Charts, it becomes easy to monitor the performance of their company even if the 
Business Owner doesn't possess any technical background . 
G. Tracking Orders with QR Code  
 When an MSME receives an order, a specific 
QR code will be created and distributed to both parties at the 
time of the order confirmation. Each of the two parties can 
scan the QR code with the camera of a mobile device, thus 
giving them both the ability to obtain real -time status 
information on the order, in addition to current states of the 
order, as the system will reach back to the cloud database to 
obtain the requested information. This eliminates the need 
for users to renew their logins repeatedly or r equest 
information by other means, while at the same time allowing 
both the MSME and the buyer to have complete visibility of 
the order during the fulfillment process.  
H. AI-Based Market Analysis  
 The AI based market analysis systems are 
designed to help both MSMEs and their customer base better 
understand current demand for products as well as identify 
price trends by examining trends in both market and 
transactional data. Analysis results will pro vide information 
on current and future prices of products by presenting these 
insights to the user through a dashboard interface, thereby 
streamlining the amount of time needed for the user to 
develop the information without having to conduct manual 
resea rch on markets or prices for a product.  
 
Pilot deployment observations indicate that web 
response times are below 1 second when the database is 
loaded normally and that database queries remain relatively 
low latency. Additionally, multisite/multiuser access has 
been shown to be consistent and sta ble for MSME 
environments. There is consistent output generation across 
all modules (forecasting, recommendations, market insights) 
demonstrating usability and operational efficiency in small -
to-medium cluster deployments  
 
VII. TESTING AND EVALUATION  
TexConnect  has been tested on low 
specification Android phones on 2G and 3G networks 
using the same simulated environment as that of the 
industry environment. Test results show dashboard 
average response time was 1.8 seconds, with no more than 
2.5 seconds for real -time database synchronisation delays 
when many users are simultaneously accessing database 
updates. The use of QR -based bundle tracking allowed 
verification to be done with phones rather than through 
traditional bundle verification methods, reducing the 
number of successful verifications by almost 40%. In Proceedings of the 9th International Conference on Intelligent Computing and Control Systems (ICICCS-2026)

 estimating how well the AI forecasting models work, the 
accuracy of the AI model based on historical order trends 
for short -term orders, was 82%. Participants from 12 
MSMEs report improved clarity of their work processes 
as a result of being able to see th e status of their orders. 
Non-native English speakers benefited greatly from the 
bilingual interface by being able to complete usability 
tasks in a timely manner.  
 
VIII. RESULTS AND DISCUSSION  
 TexConnect increased the transparency of 
workflows, reduced stock mismatches, expedited the 
updates to orders, and allowed buyers to follow progress. 
The system performed well in low bandwidth environments. 
Acceptance users reported high satisfaction with  a bilingual 
user interface and a simple design.  
 
As shown  in Fig. 5: The Buyer Module will provide structure on how buyers 
can place orders, track their status, and communicate with MSMEs.  
 
 
As shown  in Fig. 6: The MSME inventory management interface provides 
real-time updates of stock and offers a simple bilingual dashboard.  
 
 
As shown  in Fig. 7: The buyer dashboard will allow for order tracking, 
status checking, and communication with verified MSMEs.   
As shown  in Fig. 8: Admin Verification Panel will allow for administrator 
approval of MSME registration and monitoring of activities on the platform.  
 
IX. CONCLUSION  
 TexConnect shows that a bilingual cloud -
based software platform can change the digital workflows 
for textile MSMEs in Tiruppur. With TexConnect, 
communication can be improved, buyer -supplier 
transparency can be enhanced, and end users can benefit 
from a re gional user experience through a bilingual interface 
that reflects local norms and practices. With enough 
expansion, TexConnect can become a scalable model for the 
digitization of MSMEs across India .
